  2991  (2306)      INTEGER : lastTopicL := 0 - 1
  2992  (2307)      BYTES : lastRoom(0:19)
  2993  (2308)  
  2994  (2309)      % THE TYPED LINE ONLY CHANGES WHEN IT IS TYPED IN. Everything else that
  2995  (2310)      % repaints still has to put the CURSOR back - SINTRAN echoes at wherever
  2996  (2311)      % the cursor happens to be - but parking the cursor costs a few bytes and
  2997  (2312)      % rewriting the whole row costs eighty.
  2998  (2313)      BOOLEAN : inputDirty := TRUE
  2999  (2314)  
  3000  (2315)      % One line under construction.
  3001  (2316)      BYTES : lineBuf(0:76)
  3002  (2317)      INTEGER ARRAY : cal(0:6)
  3003  (2318)  
  3004  (2319)      % ---- THE INPUT LINE --------------------------------------------------
  3005  (2320)      % 76 columns, physical 4..79 on row 22.
  3006  (2321)      CONSTANT inpMax=76
  3007  (2322)      CONSTANT inpRow=2            % LOCAL row inside frame C - physical 22
  3008  (2323)      CONSTANT inpCol=4
  3009  (2324)  
  3010  (2325)      % DOES THIS ROOM REACH PAST THIS MACHINE?
  3011  (2326)      %
  3012  (2327)      % Set when a speaker arrives whose name carries an @ - which is the server
  3013  (2328)      % saying "this one is on another machine". Derived from real traffic, not
  3014  (2329)      % from a table this client would have to keep in step with the server's.
  3015  (2330)      %
  3016  (2331)      % It does not go back to FALSE on its own. A room that HAS been trunked is
  3017  (2332)      % still a trunked room when the other side goes quiet, and flickering the
  3018  (2333)      % marker off every idle minute would be worse than leaving it on.
  3019  (2334)      BOOLEAN : sawRemote := FALSE
  3020  (2335)  
  3021  (2336)      % ---- SINGLE CHARACTERS -----------------------------------------------
  3022  (2337)      % A STRING is not a BYTE in PLANC. 'x' =: buf(i) does not compile, and the
  3023  (2338)      % error names the ARRAY, which sends you to the declaration where nothing
  3024  (2339)      % is wrong.
  3025  (2340)      BYTES : digits := '0123456789'
  3026  (2341)      % spaceByte is NOT declared again here - the client already has one,
  3027  (2342)      % and a second draws IDENTIFIER ALREADY SPECIFIED/DECLARED.
  3028  (2343)      BYTES : colonByte := ':'
  3029  (2344)      BYTES : starByte := '*'
  3030  (2345)      BYTES : atByte := '@'
  3031  (2346)  
  3032  (2347)      % MON3's echo table. Ignored for every strategy except 7; it exists
  3033  (2348)      % because the call takes three arguments.
  3034  (2349)      BOOLEAN ARRAY PACKED : echoTab(0:127)
  3035  (2350)  
  3036  (2351)      INTEGER4 : termType
  3037  (2352)      INTEGER4 : termKind
  3038  (2353)  
  3039  (2354)      % ---- WHO WE ARE, and the room --------------------------------------
  3040  (2355)      % PSEUDO-KEYS. Returned by pollKey for things that arrive as escape
  3041  (2356)      % sequences, so the main loop never has to know about them.
  3042  (2357)      % VTM'S OWN KEY CODES, MEASURED ON D100 2026-08-25 with KEYPROB.PLNC.
  3043  (2358)      % These are what VTINBT hands back after it has decoded whatever the
  3044  (2359)      % terminal actually sent, so they are the same on a TDV as on a VT100.
  3045  (2360)      %
  3046  (2361)      % Note the cursor keys are SMALL numbers, not high ones - so a rule like
  3047  (2362)      % "anything over 127 is a function key" would be wrong. They are ND
  3048  (2363)      % control codes, and a printable character is its ordinary ASCII value
  3049  (2364)      % with the parity bit already stripped by VTM.
  3050  (2365)      CONSTANT keyPgUp=201
  3051  (2366)      CONSTANT keyPgDn=197
  3052  (2367)      CONSTANT keyUp=28
  3053  (2368)      CONSTANT keyDown=11
  3054  (2369)  
  3055  (2370)      % =====================================================================
  3056  (2371)      % WINDOWS - VTM's own viewport. Absolute coordinates ALWAYS: VTDSCR does
  3057  (2372)      % not nest, and a second call reads its rectangle against the physical
  3058  (2373)      % screen rather than against the current one.
  3059  (2374)      % =====================================================================
  3060  (2375)  
  3061  (2376)      % TRUE only on a terminal that can address its cursor. Decided ONCE
  3062  (2377)      % at start-up from the CTYTP bits and never asked again - a renderer
  3063  (2378)      % that could change under the program would need every drawing site
  3064  (2379)      % to cope with both, which is how a screen ends up half-painted.
  3065  (2380)      %
  3066  (2381)      % FALSE for now. This increment brings the code across and proves it
  3067  (2382)      % compiles, links and fits; nothing calls it yet and the client
  3068  (2383)      % behaves exactly as it did before.
  3069  (2384)      % HOW MANY ARE IN THE ROOM. Zero until a /who answer has been counted -
  3070  (2385)      % CHATUI had this as the literal 14, which is exactly the kind of invented
  3071  (2386)      % number this merge removes. INTEGER4 because intdis takes a double word.
  3072  (2387)      INTEGER4 : hereCount := 0
  3073  (2388)  
  3074  (2389)      BOOLEAN : screenOn := FALSE
  3075  (2390)  
  3076  (2391)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : winEnter(row, col, high, wide)
  3077  (2392)          INTEGER : st
  3078  (2393)          VTDSCR(row, col, row + high - 1, col + wide - 1) =: st
  3079  (2394)      ENDROUTINE
  3080  (2395)  
  3081  (2396)      % Back out to the whole screen. -1 is "to the end", the same convention
  3082  (2397)      % IVTCSCR(1,1,-1,-1) uses. VTINIT is not the only way back.
  3083  (2398)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : winLeave
  3084  (2399)          INTEGER : st
  3085  (2400)          VTDSCR(1, 1, -1, -1) =: st
  3086  (2401)      ENDROUTINE
  3087  (2402)  
  3088  (2403)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : flush
  3089  (2404)          INTEGER : st
  3090  (2405)          VTWBUF =: st
  3091  (2406)      ENDROUTINE
  3092  (2407)  
  3093  (2408)      % =====================================================================
  3094  (2409)      % SMALL HELPERS
  3095  (2410)      % =====================================================================
  3096  (2411)  
  3097  (2412)      % Two digits into a buffer at a given offset.
  3098  (2413)      %
  3099  (2414)      % CLAMPED INSIDE, not at the call sites. MN113's year element is the FULL
  3100  (2415)      % year - 1998, not 98 - and digits(1998/10) is digits(199), a read 190
  3101  (2416)      % bytes past the end of a ten-byte literal. PLANC checks no array bound,
  3102  (2417)      % so it fetches whatever follows and prints a blank where a digit belongs.
  3103  (2418)      % One clamp here covers every caller.
  3104  (2419)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER) : put2(at, value)
  3105  (2420)          INTEGER : v
  3106  (2421)          value =: v
  3107  (2422)          IF v < 0 THEN
  3108  (2423)              0 =: v
  3109  (2424)          ENDIF
  3110  (2425)          v MOD 100 =: v
  3111  (2426)          digits(v / 10) =: lineBuf(at)
  3112  (2427)          digits(v MOD 10) =: lineBuf(at + 1)
  3113  (2428)      ENDROUTINE
  3114  (2429)  
  3115  (2430)      % A WHOLE NUMBER INTO lineBuf, no leading zeros, and it answers how many
  3116  (2431)      % characters it used so the caller can go on writing after it.
  3117  (2432)      %
  3118  (2433)      % put2 above is fixed at two digits because a clock field is always two.
  3119  (2434)      % An unread count is not: it is usually one digit, and a window left alone
  3120  (2435)      % all afternoon can reach four. Padding it to a fixed width would put a
  3121  (2436)      % ragged gap in the middle of the bar.
  3122  (2437)      %
  3123  (2438)      % CLAMPED AT 9999. Nothing sensible counts higher, and a fifth digit would
  3124  (2439)      % need a fifth byte in the scratch below - which PLANC would not check.
  3125  (2440)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : putNum(at, value)
  3126  (2441)          INTEGER : v
  3127  (2442)          INTEGER : n
  3128  (2443)          INTEGER : i
  3129  (2444)          BYTES : scratch(0:3)
  3130  (2445)  
  3131  (2446)          value =: v
  3132  (2447)          IF v < 0 THEN
  3133  (2448)              0 =: v
  3134  (2449)          ENDIF
  3135  (2450)          IF v > 9999 THEN
  3136  (2451)              9999 =: v
  3137  (2452)          ENDIF
  3138  (2453)  
  3139  (2454)          % Digits come out backwards, so they are held and then written in
  3140  (2455)          % reverse. A zero has to be handled on its own - the loop below would
  3141  (2456)          % produce nothing at all for it.
  3142  (2457)          0 =: n
  3143  (2458)          IF v = 0 THEN
  3144  (2459)              digits(0) =: lineBuf(at)
  3145  (2460)              1 RETURN
  3146  (2461)          ENDIF
  3147  (2462)  
  3148  (2463)          DO
  3149  (2464)              WHILE v > 0
  3150  (2465)              digits(v MOD 10) =: scratch(n)
  3151  (2466)              n + 1 =: n
  3152  (2467)              v / 10 =: v
  3153  (2468)          ENDDO
  3154  (2469)  
  3155  (2470)          FOR i IN 1:n DO
  3156  (2471)              scratch(n - i) =: lineBuf(at + i - 1)
  3157  (2472)          ENDFOR
  3158  (2473)          n RETURN
  3159  (2474)      ENDROUTINE
  3160  (2475)  
  3161  (2476)      % Blank the whole line buffer. Every stored line is PADDED to full width
  3162  (2477)      % so a short line painted over a long one cannot leave the old tail
  3163  (2478)      % behind - that removes the need for a separate erase before each paint.
  3164  (2479)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : blankLine
  3165  (2480)          INTEGER : i
  3166  (2481)          FOR i IN 0:lineWide - 1 DO
  3167  (2482)              spaceByte(0) =: lineBuf(i)
  3168  (2483)          ENDFOR
  3169  (2484)      ENDROUTINE
  3170  (2485)  
  3171  (2486)      % Copy text into the line buffer at an offset, up to a field width, and
  3172  (2487)      % answer how many characters were taken. MAXINDEX asks the array its own
  3173  (2488)      % declared bound, so no length is ever typed beside a literal - a
  3174  (2489)      % hand-counted length builds clean and shows only as a line in the wrong
  3175  (2490)      % place.
  3176  (2491)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER, BYTES) : putField(at, wide, text)
  3177  (2492)          INTEGER : i
  3178  (2493)          INTEGER : n
  3179  (2494)          MAXINDEX(text, 1) + 1 =: n
  3180  (2495)          IF n > wide THEN
  3181  (2496)              wide =: n
  3182  (2497)          ENDIF
  3183  (2498)          IF n > 0 THEN
  3184  (2499)              FOR i IN 0:n - 1 DO
  3185  (2500)                  text(i) =: lineBuf(at + i)
  3186  (2501)              ENDFOR
  3187  (2502)          ENDIF
  3188  (2503)          n RETURN
  3189  (2504)      ENDROUTINE
  3190  (2505)  
  3191  (2506)      % =====================================================================
  3192  (2507)      % THE SPEAKER COLUMN - 13 characters against a name that may be longer
  3193  (2508)      %
  3194  (2509)      % THE NAME ARRIVES ALREADY DECIDED. The server qualifies a speaker on
  3195  (2510)      % another machine as ANNA@D102 and leaves a local one bare, so this client
  3196  (2511)      % never has to work out which is which - and never has to know which
  3197  (2512)      % machine it is on, which it has no way of finding out.
  3198  (2513)      %
  3199  (2514)      % An earlier version of this DID decide for itself, from a nickname and a
  3200  (2515)      % system name it had been handed as two literals. That is exactly the kind
  3201  (2516)      % of invented state this merge exists to remove.
  3202  (2517)      %
  3203  (2518)      % What is left is the fitting rule, and it still matters: 13 columns
  3204  (2519)      % against a name that may be 16 plus @D102.
  3205  (2520)      %
  3206  (2521)      %   fits            copied as it is
  3207  (2522)      %   too long, no @  cut at 13
  3208  (2523)      %   too long, @     THE NAME IS CUT, NEVER THE MACHINE
  3209  (2524)      %
  3210  (2525)      % Cutting the machine would be the wrong choice: inside one room you know
  3211  (2526)      % a person from the first letters of their name, but D10 does not tell you
  3212  (2527)      % whether the line came from D100, D102 or D103 - and on a trunked room
  3213  (2528)      % that is the one thing this column is there for.
  3214  (2529)      % =====================================================================
  3215  (2530)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (BYTES) : putWho(who)
  3216  (2531)          INTEGER : total
  3217  (2532)          INTEGER : i
  3218  (2533)          INTEGER : bang
  3219  (2534)          INTEGER : tail
  3220  (2535)          INTEGER : room
  3221  (2536)  
  3222  (2537)          MAXINDEX(who, 1) + 1 =: total
  3223  (2538)  
  3224  (2539)          IF total <= widWho THEN
  3225  (2540)              FOR i IN 0:total - 1 DO
  3226  (2541)                  who(i) =: lineBuf(offWho + i)
  3227  (2542)              ENDFOR
  3228  (2543)              RETURN
  3229  (2544)          ENDIF
  3230  (2545)  
  3231  (2546)          % Where the machine starts, if there is one.
  3232  (2547)          0 - 1 =: bang
  3233  (2548)          FOR i IN 0:total - 1 DO
  3234  (2549)              IF bang < 0 THEN
  3235  (2550)                  IF who(i) = atByte(0) THEN
  3236  (2551)                      i =: bang
  3237  (2552)                  ENDIF
  3238  (2553)              ENDIF
  3239  (2554)          ENDFOR
  3240  (2555)  
  3241  (2556)          IF bang < 0 THEN
  3242  (2557)              % No machine in it. Cut the name.
  3243  (2558)              FOR i IN 0:widWho - 1 DO
  3244  (2559)                  who(i) =: lineBuf(offWho + i)
  3245  (2560)              ENDFOR
  3246  (2561)              RETURN
  3247  (2562)          ENDIF
  3248  (2563)  
  3249  (2564)          % "@D102" and everything after it is kept whole; the name gets what is
  3250  (2565)          % left. If the machine alone fills the field there is no name shown -
  3251  (2566)          % which machine still beats a single initial.
  3252  (2567)          total - bang =: tail
  3253  (2568)          widWho - tail =: room
  3254  (2569)          IF room < 0 THEN
  3255  (2570)              0 =: room
  3256  (2571)          ENDIF
  3257  (2572)  
  3258  (2573)          IF room > 0 THEN
  3259  (2574)              FOR i IN 0:room - 1 DO
  3260  (2575)                  who(i) =: lineBuf(offWho + i)
  3261  (2576)              ENDFOR
  3262  (2577)          ENDIF
  3263  (2578)          FOR i IN 0:tail - 1 DO
  3264  (2579)              IF room + i < widWho THEN
  3265  (2580)                  who(bang + i) =: lineBuf(offWho + room + i)
  3266  (2581)              ENDIF
  3267  (2582)          ENDFOR
  3268  (2583)      ENDROUTINE
  3269  (2584)  
  3270  (2585)      % THE CURRENT WINDOW'S COUNTERS GO BACK INTO ITS ROW.
  3271  (2586)      %
  3272  (2587)      % cacheCount, cacheFirst and viewFrom are the WORKING copies - every
  3273  (2588)      % routine that paints or adds reads those three names. Keeping them as
  3274  (2589)      % scalars is what let windows be added without touching any of that code.
  3275  (2590)      % The price is that they must be put back before another window is loaded,
  3276  (2591)      % and that is what these two routines are for. They are always used as a
  3277  (2592)      % pair; a save without a load leaves two windows sharing one ring.
  3278  (2593)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : winSave
  3279  (2594)          cacheCount =: winCount(curWin)
  3280  (2595)          cacheFirst =: winFirst(curWin)
  3281  (2596)          viewFrom   =: winView(curWin)
  3282  (2597)      ENDROUTINE
  3283  (2598)  
  3284  (2599)      % MAKE A WINDOW THE WORKING ONE. This does NOT clear its unread count -
  3285  (2600)      % it is used both by a person switching to a window and by the delivery
  3286  (2601)      % path stepping into one for long enough to drop a line in. Only the
  3287  (2602)      % first of those means "you have seen it", so the clearing lives in
  3288  (2603)      % winGo where the person actually is.
  3289  (2604)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER) : winUse(w)
  3290  (2605)          w =: curWin
  3291  (2606)          winCount(w) =: cacheCount
  3292  (2607)          winFirst(w) =: cacheFirst
  3293  (2608)          winView(w)  =: viewFrom
  3294  (2609)      ENDROUTINE
  3295  (2610)  
  3296  (2611)      % What the bar calls a window. Stored WITHOUT the leading = so it can be
  3297  (2612)      % compared with a name that arrives off the wire; the = is drawn.
  3298  (2613)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : winName(w, at, n)
  3299  (2614)          INTEGER : i
  3300  (2615)          INTEGER : take
  3301  (2616)  
  3302  (2617)          n =: take
  3303  (2618)          IF take > 16 THEN
  3304  (2619)              16 =: take
  3305  (2620)          ENDIF
  3306  (2621)          IF take < 0 THEN
  3307  (2622)              0 =: take
  3308  (2623)          ENDIF
  3309  (2624)          IF take > 0 THEN
  3310  (2625)              FOR i IN 1:take DO
  3311  (2626)                  inBuf(at + i - 1) =: winLabel(w * 16 + i - 1)
  3312  (2627)              ENDFOR
  3313  (2628)          ENDIF
  3314  (2629)          take =: winLabLen(w)
  3315  (2630)      ENDROUTINE
  3316  (2631)  
  3317  (2632)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3318  (2633)      % The same message, but addressed to a WINDOW rather than to a name the
  3319  (2634)      % user typed.
  3320  (2635)      %
  3321  (2636)      % This is what makes a conversation window behave like a conversation: the
  3322  (2637)      % target comes from the window's own label, so a plain line typed there
  3323  (2638)      % goes to that person and nowhere else. buildTell above cannot serve this
  3324  (2639)      % because its target is a run of characters inside `typed`, and here the
  3325  (2640)      % whole of `typed` is the message.
  3326  (2641)      %
  3327  (2642)      % The wire shape is identical - "TARGET/message" - so a server that has
  3328  (2643)      % never heard of windows sees an ordinary direct message.
  3329  (2644)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3330  (2645)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : buildTellTo(w, msgLen)
  3331  (2646)          INTEGER : i, at, tgtLen
  3332  (2647)  
  3333  (2648)          winLabLen(w) =: tgtLen
  3334  (2649)  
  3335  (2650)          kDirect =: outBuf(0)
  3336  (2651)          lenMyName =: outBuf(1)
  3337  (2652)          2 =: at
  3338  (2653)          FOR i IN 1:lenMyName DO
  3339  (2654)              myName(i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  3340  (2655)              at + 1 =: at
  3341  (2656)          ENDFOR
  3342  (2657)  
  3343  (2658)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  3344  (2659)          tgtLen + 1 + msgLen =: outBuf(at + 1)
  3345  (2660)          at + 2 =: at
  3346  (2661)  
  3347  (2662)          FOR i IN 1:tgtLen DO
  3348  (2663)              winLabel(w * 16 + i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  3349  (2664)              at + 1 =: at
  3350  (2665)          ENDFOR
  3351  (2666)          slashByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  3352  (2667)          at + 1 =: at
  3353  (2668)          FOR i IN 1:msgLen DO
  3354  (2669)              typed(i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  3355  (2670)              at + 1 =: at
  3356  (2671)          ENDFOR
  3357  (2672)          at RETURN
  3358  (2673)      ENDROUTINE
  3359  (2674)  
  3360  (2675)      % EMPTY THE ROOM. Used when this client moves to another one.
  3361  (2676)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : cacheClear
  3362  (2677)          0 =: cacheCount
  3363  (2678)          0 =: cacheFirst
  3364  (2679)          0 =: viewFrom
  3365  (2680)      ENDROUTINE
  3366  (2681)  
  3367  (2682)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : cacheAdd
  3368  (2683)          INTEGER : slot
  3369  (2684)          INTEGER : i
  3370  (2685)          INTEGER : base
  3371  (2686)  
  3372  (2687)          % BOUNDED BY winLines, NOT cacheLines. cacheLines is the whole array
  3373  (2688)          % across every window; a window counting up to it would walk straight
  3374  (2689)          % through its neighbour's lines. PLANC checks no bound, so nothing
  3375  (2690)          % would say so - the other window's history would simply start
  3376  (2691)          % changing under it.
  3377  (2692)          IF cacheCount >= winLines THEN
  3378  (2693)              % Full: the oldest slot is reused and becomes the newest.
  3379  (2694)              cacheFirst =: slot
  3380  (2695)              cacheFirst + 1 =: cacheFirst
  3381  (2696)              IF cacheFirst >= winLines THEN
  3382  (2697)                  0 =: cacheFirst
  3383  (2698)              ENDIF
  3384  (2699)          ELSE
  3385  (2700)              cacheCount =: slot
  3386  (2701)              cacheCount + 1 =: cacheCount
  3387  (2702)          ENDIF
  3388  (2703)  
  3389  (2704)          % ONE MORE LINE HAS EXISTED IN THIS WINDOW, ever. This is what gives
  3390  (2705)          % the line a number the painter can compare a row against - see
  3391  (2706)          % rowShown. It counts lines, not messages, because a wrapped message
  3392  (2707)          % becomes several lines and each occupies a row of its own.
  3393  (2708)          winTotal(curWin) + 1 =: winTotal(curWin)
  3394  (2709)  
  3395  (2710)          % The slice this window owns starts here. The slot above is WITHIN the
  3396  (2711)          % window; this is the only place the two are added.
  3397  (2712)          ((curWin - 1) * winLines + slot) * lineWide =: base
  3398  (2713)          FOR i IN 0:lineWide - 1 DO
  3399  (2714)              lineBuf(i) =: cache(base + i)
  3400  (2715)          ENDFOR
  3401  (2716)  
  3402  (2717)          % If the user is paged back, one more line has appeared BELOW them, so
  3403  (2718)          % the distance to the newest grows by one - otherwise the view would
  3404  (2719)          % drift forward a line every time somebody spoke.
  3405  (2720)          IF viewFrom > 0 THEN
  3406  (2721)              viewFrom + 1 =: viewFrom
  3407  (2722)              % ...but never past the oldest line we still hold.
  3408  (2723)              IF viewFrom > cacheCount - msgRows THEN
  3409  (2724)                  cacheCount - msgRows =: viewFrom
  3410  (2725)              ENDIF
  3411  (2726)              IF viewFrom < 0 THEN
  3412  (2727)                  0 =: viewFrom
  3413  (2728)              ENDIF
  3414  (2729)          ENDIF
  3415  (2730)      ENDROUTINE
  3416  (2731)  
  3417  (2732)      % Which cache slot holds the line at a given position, counted from the
  3418  (2733)      % OLDEST held line. The ring is unwound in ONE place, here.
  3419  (2734)      % ABSOLUTE, so the caller can index the array straight away. It wraps
  3420  (2735)      % WITHIN the window and only then adds the window's offset - the other way
  3421  (2736)      % round would wrap one window's last line onto the next window's first.
  3422  (2737)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER) : slotAt(index)
  3423  (2738)          INTEGER : s
  3424  (2739)          cacheFirst + index =: s
  3425  (2740)          IF s >= winLines THEN
  3426  (2741)              s - winLines =: s
  3427  (2742)          ENDIF
  3428  (2743)          (curWin - 1) * winLines + s RETURN
  3429  (2744)      ENDROUTINE
  3430  (2745)  
  3431  (2746)      % ---- THE ARRIVAL INSTRUMENT, behind a compile-time switch --------------
  3432  (2747)      %
  3433  (2748)      % Five numbers on the TITLE row, repainted on every arrival that was
  3434  (2749)      % shown: the kind, the read length, the first text byte OF THE ARRIVAL,
  3435  (2750)      % cacheCount, and the first text byte of the NEWEST cache row. One glance
  3436  (2751)      % separates "the arriving bytes are stale" (third number wrong) from "the
  3437  (2752)      % store went stale" (third right, fifth wrong) - which is exactly how the
  3438  (2753)      % repeat-first-message bug was pinned to the painter on 2026-09-01: it
  3439  (2754)      % read 005 015 097 003 097 while the screen showed the wrong line, so the
  3440  (2755)      % wire and the cache were right and only the paint was wrong.
  3441  (2756)      %
  3442  (2757)      % $CONSTANT DBGUI is PLANC's #ifdef (ND-60.117.5 appendix A 0.9/0.10):
  3443  (2758)      % set it to 0 and the instrument compiles out entirely - code, buffer and
  3444  (2759)      % call sites. It stays IN the source either way, by Ronny's decision.
  3445  (2760)  $CONSTANT DBGUI=0
  3446  (2761)  $IF DBGUI = 1 $THEN
  3447       .      BYTES : dbgRow(0:21)
  3448       .  
  3449       .      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER) : dbgPut3(at, v)
  3450       .          INTEGER : x
  3451       .          v =: x
  3452       .          IF x < 0 THEN
  3453       .              0 - x =: x
  3454       .          ENDIF
  3455       .          x - ((x / 1000) * 1000) =: x
  3456       .          digits(x / 100) =: dbgRow(at)
  3457       .          x - ((x / 100) * 100) =: x
  3458       .          digits(x / 10) =: dbgRow(at + 1)
  3459       .          digits(x - ((x / 10) * 10)) =: dbgRow(at + 2)
  3460       .      ENDROUTINE
  3461       .  
  3462       .      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : dbgShow(k, rl, t0)
  3463       .          INTEGER : i, base, c0
  3464       .          FOR i IN 0:21 DO
  3465       .              spaceByte(0) =: dbgRow(i)
  3466       .          ENDFOR
  3467       .          dbgPut3(0, k)
  3468       .          dbgPut3(4, rl)
  3469       .          dbgPut3(8, t0)
  3470       .          dbgPut3(12, cacheCount)
  3471       .          0 =: c0
  3472       .          IF cacheCount > 0 THEN
  3473       .              slotAt(cacheCount - 1) * lineWide + offText =: base
  3474       .              cache(base) =: c0
  3475       .          ENDIF
  3476       .          dbgPut3(16, c0)
  3477       .          IF screenOn THEN
  3478       .              bytdis(1, 56, 19, dbgRow(0:18), '')
  3479       .          ENDIF
  3480       .      ENDROUTINE
  3481       .  $ENDIF
  3482  (2797)  
  3483  (2798)      % =====================================================================
  3484  (2799)      % THE ROOM'S MEMBERS, and the one question they answer
  3485  (2800)      % =====================================================================
  3486  (2801)  
  3487  (2802)      % =====================================================================
  3488  (2803)      % ADDING A LINE, WITH WRAPPING
  3489  (2804)      %
  3490  (2805)      % 57 columns of text is narrow and a real sentence overruns it. A line that
  3491  (2806)      % is simply CUT loses what somebody said, silently, which is the worst of
  3492  (2807)      % the three options.
  3493  (2808)      %
  3494  (2809)      % So a long line becomes SEVERAL cache lines: the first carries the time and
  3495  (2810)      % the speaker, and every continuation carries neither. That is what makes a
  3496  (2811)      % wrapped line readable as one utterance rather than as two people talking -
  3497  (2812)      % the eye follows the empty speaker column down.
  3498  (2813)      %
  3499  (2814)      % Each continuation is a cache line in its OWN right, so paging and the ring
  3500  (2815)      % need no special case at all. The cost is that a very long message uses
  3501  (2816)      % several of the 300 slots, which is correct: they ARE several screen lines.
  3502  (2817)      % =====================================================================
  3503  (2818)  
  3504  (2819)      % Put one row into the cache: a time and speaker only on the first.
  3505  (2820)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, BYTES, BYTES, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  3506  (2821)              pushRow(withHead, who, text, from, count)
  3507  (2822)          INTEGER : i
  3508  (2823)  
  3509  (2824)          blankLine
  3510  (2825)          IF withHead = 1 THEN
  3511  (2826)              % NOT WRITING IT IS HOW IT IS BLANKED - blankLine has already
  3512  (2827)              % cleared the row to spaces, so a time nobody recorded simply
  3513  (2828)              % leaves the column empty instead of asserting a moment.
  3514  (2829)              IF timeKnown THEN
  3515  (2830)                  put2(offTime, cal(3))
  3516  (2831)                  colonByte(0) =: lineBuf(offTime + 2)
  3517  (2832)                  put2(offTime + 3, cal(2))
  3518  (2833)              ENDIF
  3519  (2834)              putWho(who)
  3520  (2835)          ENDIF
  3521  (2836)  
  3522  (2837)          % The slice of the message this row carries. Copied byte by byte rather
  3523  (2838)          % than with a subarray, because the bounds are computed and a subarray
  3524  (2839)          % whose bounds fall outside the original breaks the compiler's own
  3525  (2840)          % index checking - appendix G item 22.
  3526  (2841)          IF count > 0 THEN
  3527  (2842)              FOR i IN 0:count - 1 DO
  3528  (2843)                  text(from + i) =: lineBuf(offText + i)
  3529  (2844)              ENDFOR
  3530  (2845)          ENDIF
  3531  (2846)          cacheAdd
  3532  (2847)      ENDROUTINE
  3533  (2848)  
  3534  (2849)      % Add what somebody said, wrapped across as many rows as it needs.
  3535  (2850)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (BYTES, BYTES) : addSaid(who, text)
  3536  (2851)          INTEGER : total
  3537  (2852)          INTEGER : at
  3538  (2853)          INTEGER : take
  3539  (2854)          INTEGER : head
  3540  (2855)  
  3541  (2856)          % A REPLAYED LINE IS STAMPED WITH WHEN IT WAS SAID, not with now.
  3542  (2857)          % The two calendar elements pushRow reads are filled from the wire
  3543  (2858)          % instead of from the clock; everything downstream is unchanged.
  3544  (2859)          IF msgIsHist THEN
  3545  (2860)              histHour =: cal(3)
  3546  (2861)              histMin =: cal(2)
  3547  (2862)          ELSE
  3548  (2863)              MN113(cal)
  3549  (2864)          ENDIF
  3550  (2865)  
  3551  (2866)          % AN @ IN THE NAME IS THE SERVER SAYING "another machine", and that is
  3552  (2867)          % all the @trunk marker needs.
  3553  (2868)          FOR at IN 0:MAXINDEX(who, 1) DO
  3554  (2869)              IF who(at) = atByte(0) THEN
  3555  (2870)                  TRUE =: sawRemote
  3556  (2871)              ENDIF
  3557  (2872)          ENDFOR
  3558  (2873)  
  3559  (2874)          MAXINDEX(text, 1) + 1 =: total
  3560  (2875)          0 =: at
  3561  (2876)          1 =: head
  3562  (2877)  
  3563  (2878)          % A DO loop with the test inside it, because PLANC has no while-header.
  3564  (2879)          % The first pass runs even for an empty message, so a blank line still
  3565  (2880)          % appears with its time - which is what a user expects if somebody
  3566  (2881)          % presses RETURN on nothing.
  3567  (2882)          DO
  3568  (2883)              total - at =: take
  3569  (2884)              IF take > widText THEN
  3570  (2885)                  widText =: take
  3571  (2886)              ENDIF
  3572  (2887)              pushRow(head, who, text, at, take)
  3573  (2888)              0 =: head
  3574  (2889)              at + take =: at
  3575  (2890)              WHILE at < total
  3576  (2891)          ENDDO
  3577  (2892)      ENDROUTINE
  3578  (2893)  
  3579  (2894)      % =====================================================================
  3580  (2895)      % CLIPPING - because VTM will not do it for us
  3581  (2896)      %
  3582  (2897)      % The viewport clips a WRITE to the window it is in, but it knows nothing
  3583  (2898)      % about a window drawn ON TOP of that one. So overlap is ours to handle,
  3584  (2899)      % and there are exactly two honest ways to do it: paint only the parts
  3585  (2900)      % that are visible, or paint nothing at all.
  3586  (2901)      %
  3587  (2902)      % PAINTING NOTHING IS THE ONE THAT LOOKS SAFE AND IS WRONG. It was what
  3588  (2903)      % this file did - "IF NOT panelUp THEN paint" - and it freezes the whole
  3589  (2904)      % room to protect the few rows a centred window actually covers. TESTUI
  3590  (2905)      % made the same mistake first and Ronny reported it there in the same
  3591  (2906)      % words: the buffer updates and the screen does not.
  3592  (2907)      %
  3593  (2908)      % A cell-by-cell test is NOT expensive. Seventy-seven tests a row is
  3594  (2909)      % nothing on the CPU, and it SAVES time on the wire, which is the slow
  3595  (2910)      % part - only the visible runs are sent.
  3596  (2911)      % =====================================================================
  3597  (2912)  
  3598  (2913)      % Is one PHYSICAL cell visible, or is the panel over it?
  3599  (2914)      ROUTINE VOID, BOOLEAN (INTEGER, INTEGER) : visibleAt(prow, pcol)
  3600  (2915)          IF panelUp THEN
  3601  (2916)              IF prow >= panRow AND prow <= panRow + panHigh - 1 THEN
  3602  (2917)                  IF pcol >= panCol AND pcol <= panCol + panWid - 1 THEN
  3603  (2918)                      FALSE RETURN
  3604  (2919)                  ENDIF
  3605  (2920)              ENDIF
  3606  (2921)          ENDIF
  3607  (2922)          TRUE RETURN
  3608  (2923)      ENDROUTINE
  3609  (2924)  
  3610  (2925)      % Does the panel cover any part of this PHYSICAL row?
  3611  (2926)      %
  3612  (2927)      % Deliberately coarser than visibleAt: it answers for the whole row, not a
  3613  (2928)      % cell. The one-row things - the status line, the bar, the input line - are
  3614  (2929)      % drawn by routines that lay out a whole row at once, so the useful
  3615  (2930)      % question about them is "leave it alone this time?" and not "which
  3616  (2931)      % columns?". Being conservative here costs one row on the close and cannot
  3617  (2932)      % draw over the panel.
  3618  (2933)      ROUTINE VOID, BOOLEAN (INTEGER) : rowCovered(prow)
  3619  (2934)          IF NOT panelUp THEN
  3620  (2935)              FALSE RETURN
  3621  (2936)          ENDIF
  3622  (2937)          IF prow >= panRow AND prow <= panRow + panHigh - 1 THEN
  3623  (2938)              TRUE RETURN
  3624  (2939)          ENDIF
  3625  (2940)          FALSE RETURN
  3626  (2941)      ENDROUTINE
  3627  (2942)  
  3628  (2943)      % Paint ONE ROW of the message pane, showing only what is not covered.
  3629  (2944)      % Walks the row once and emits each visible run as it ends.
  3630  (2945)      %
  3631  (2946)      % localRow is the mid window's own row; the window starts at column 1, so a
  3632  (2947)      % window column IS a physical column and only the row needs converting.
  3633  (2948)      %
  3634  (2949)      % THE WIDTH IS PASSED TO bytdis, NEVER 0. Width 0 means "the string says
  3635  (2950)      % its own length", which is right for a literal and wrong for a subarray -
  3636  (2951)      % measured on D100, a subarray painted with 0 ran off the right-hand side
  3637  (2952)      % and wiped two window borders. planc-lint refuses the shape now.
  3638  (2953)      %
  3639  (2954)      % THE ROW COMES IN THROUGH paintBuf, NOT A PARAMETER - 2026-09-01, and
  3640  (2955)      % this closed the repeat-first-message bug. The caller used to pass
  3641  (2956)      % cache(base : base + lineWide - 1), a subarray of the 23KB cache with a
  3642  (2957)      % COMPUTED base - and every row painted line 0's bytes while the cache
  3643  (2958)      % itself, read back by an ordinary computed INDEX, held each line's own
  3644  (2959)      % text (measured on the title-row instrument: arrival byte 97 'a', cache
  3645  (2960)      % byte 97 'a', screen showing the first line). Same family as the
  3646  (2961)      % putField subarray that compiled clean and wrote nothing at all. The
  3647  (2962)      % caller now COPIES the row into paintBuf with an explicit indexed loop -
  3648  (2963)      % the one form of cache access that is proven right - and the subarrays
  3649  (2964)      % below are of a small fixed module array, the exact shape TESTUI's
  3650  (2965)      % paintSlot has proven on this machine.
  3651  (2966)      BYTES : paintBuf(0:76)
  3652  (2967)  
  3653  (2968)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER) : paintRow(localRow)
  3654  (2969)          INTEGER : c
  3655  (2970)          INTEGER : runFrom
  3656  (2971)          INTEGER : prow
  3657  (2972)          BOOLEAN : inRun
  3658  (2973)  
  3659  (2974)          midRow + localRow - 1 =: prow
  3660  (2975)          FALSE =: inRun
  3661  (2976)          0 =: runFrom
  3662  (2977)          FOR c IN 0:lineWide - 1 DO
  3663  (2978)              IF visibleAt(prow, textCol + c) THEN
  3664  (2979)                  IF NOT inRun THEN
  3665  (2980)                      TRUE =: inRun
  3666  (2981)                      c =: runFrom
  3667  (2982)                  ENDIF
  3668  (2983)              ELSE
  3669  (2984)                  IF inRun THEN
  3670  (2985)                      bytdis(localRow, textCol + runFrom, c - runFrom, &
  3671  (2986)                             paintBuf(runFrom : c - 1), '')
  3672  (2987)                      FALSE =: inRun
  3673  (2988)                  ENDIF
  3674  (2989)              ENDIF
  3675  (2990)          ENDFOR
  3676  (2991)          % A run reaching the end of the row still has to be flushed - the loop
  3677  (2992)          % only emits one when it meets a covered cell.
  3678  (2993)          IF inRun THEN
  3679  (2994)              bytdis(localRow, textCol + runFrom, lineWide - runFrom, &
  3680  (2995)                     paintBuf(runFrom : lineWide - 1), '')
  3681  (2996)          ENDIF
  3682  (2997)      ENDROUTINE
  3683  (2998)  
  3684  (2999)  
  3685  (3000)      % =====================================================================
  3686  (3001)      % PAINTING THE MIDDLE SECTION
  3687  (3002)      % =====================================================================
  3688  (3003)  
  3689  (3004)      % Repaint the chat. This is what a page costs and it is the most
  3690  (3005)      % expensive routine here: 17 lines of 77 characters, about 1300, roughly
  3691  (3006)      % a second and a half on a real 9600-baud line.
  3692  (3007)      % WHICH LINE NUMBER BELONGS ON ROW `row` OF THE CURRENT WINDOW - or -1 if
  3693  (3008)      % the row has no line yet. Pulled out of paintChat on 2026-09-01 so this
  3694  (3009)      % arithmetic can be proved right WITHOUT a screen: it is pure integers
  3695  (3010)      % over cacheCount, winTotal, viewFrom and msgRows, and every one of those
  3696  (3011)      % is real state this same client already carries.
  3697  (3012)      %
  3698  (3013)      % ANSWERS AN ABSOLUTE LINE NUMBER, counted from the very first line this
  3699  (3014)      % window has ever held - the same number slotAt(index) expects once the
  3700  (3015)      % OLDEST-held offset is subtracted back out, which is what the two call
  3701  (3016)      % sites below already did inline.
  3702  (3017)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER) : wantForRow(row)
  3703  (3018)          INTEGER : first
  3704  (3019)          INTEGER : shown
  3705  (3020)          INTEGER : oldest
  3706  (3021)  
  3707  (3022)          cacheCount - msgRows - viewFrom =: first
  3708  (3023)          IF first < 0 THEN
  3709  (3024)              0 =: first
  3710  (3025)          ENDIF
  3711  (3026)  
  3712  (3027)          cacheCount - first =: shown
  3713  (3028)          IF shown > msgRows THEN
  3714  (3029)              msgRows =: shown
  3715  (3030)          ENDIF
  3716  (3031)  
  3717  (3032)          IF row >= shown THEN
  3718  (3033)              0 - 1 RETURN
  3719  (3034)          ENDIF
  3720  (3035)  
  3721  (3036)          winTotal(curWin) - cacheCount =: oldest
  3722  (3037)          oldest + first + row RETURN
  3723  (3038)      ENDROUTINE
  3724  (3039)  
  3725  (3040)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : paintChat
  3726  (3041)          INTEGER : i
  3727  (3042)          INTEGER : first
  3728  (3043)          INTEGER : idx
  3729  (3044)          INTEGER : base
  3730  (3045)          INTEGER : st
  3731  (3046)          INTEGER : want
  3732  (3047)  
  3733  (3048)          % NO BLANKET CLEAR ANY MORE.
  3734  (3049)          %
  3735  (3050)          % VTCREC over the whole pane blanked seventeen rows and then drew
  3736  (3051)          % seventeen back, every time one line arrived - which is both the cost
  3737  (3052)          % and the flicker. Every stored line is PADDED to the full width, so
  3738  (3053)          % writing one straight over another leaves nothing of the old one
  3739  (3054)          % behind and the erase was never needed. Rows with no line get an
  3740  (3055)          % explicit blank below, and only when they are not already blank.
  3741  (3056)          winEnter(midRow, 1, midHigh, scrWide)
  3742  (3057)  
  3743  (3058)          % Which held line goes on the top row. viewFrom counts BACK from the
  3744  (3059)          % newest, so at viewFrom = 0 the last msgRows lines are shown.
  3745  (3060)          %
  3746  (3061)          % KEPT HERE TOO, ALONGSIDE wantForRow's OWN COPY, only because
  3747  (3062)          % slotAt below wants the RELATIVE index and wantForRow hands back
  3748  (3063)          % the ABSOLUTE line number - the two callers of the same arithmetic
  3749  (3064)          % need different halves of it. wantForRow is the one that is
  3750  (3065)          % TESTED; this is the same three lines, unchanged, so the two never
  3751  (3066)          % have a reason to drift apart.
  3752  (3067)          cacheCount - msgRows - viewFrom =: first
  3753  (3068)          IF first < 0 THEN
  3754  (3069)              0 =: first
  3755  (3070)          ENDIF
  3756  (3071)  
  3757  (3072)          % THE SHADOW ONLY DESCRIBES ONE WINDOW. Line numbers are counted per
  3758  (3073)          % window, so a row holding line 12 of the room and a row holding line
  3759  (3074)          % 12 of a conversation would compare equal and the pane would keep
  3760  (3075)          % somebody else's text. Switching windows throws the shadow away.
  3761  (3076)          % INVALIDATE WITH A VALUE `want` CANNOT TAKE.
  3762  (3077)          %
  3763  (3078)          % This was minus one, and minus one is a REAL value - it is what want
  3764  (3079)          % holds for a row with no line on it. So every blank row compared
  3765  (3080)          % EQUAL to its shadow and was skipped, and switching windows left the
  3766  (3081)          % previous window's text on screen beneath the new window's first
  3767  (3082)          % line. MEASURED on D100 2026-08-26; it looked like two windows
  3768  (3083)          % sharing one buffer, which is a far more alarming thing than it was.
  3769  (3084)          %
  3770  (3085)          % Minus two is never a row number and never means blank, so nothing
  3771  (3086)          % can match it.
  3772  (3087)          IF shadowWin >< curWin THEN
  3773  (3088)              FOR i IN 0:msgRows - 1 DO
  3774  (3089)                  0 - 2 =: rowShown(i)
  3775  (3090)              ENDFOR
  3776  (3091)              curWin =: shadowWin
  3777  (3092)          ENDIF
  3778  (3093)  
  3779  (3094)          FOR i IN 0:msgRows - 1 DO
  3780  (3095)              wantForRow(i) =: want
  3781  (3096)  
  3782  (3097)              % ALREADY RIGHT? THEN LEAVE IT ALONE. This is the whole point: a
  3783  (3098)              % pane that is still filling changes ONE row per arriving line, and
  3784  (3099)              % a pane where nothing has moved changes none.
  3785  (3100)              IF rowShown(i) >< want THEN
  3786  (3101)                  % THROUGH paintRow, WHICH CLIPS. A panel over part of this row
  3787  (3102)                  % gets the covered columns skipped and the rest painted, so a
  3788  (3103)                  % line arriving while /help is open still scrolls the room
  3789  (3104)                  % underneath it. The width goes to bytdis inside paintRow.
  3790  (3105)                  %
  3791  (3106)                  % THE ROW IS COPIED INTO paintBuf BY INDEX, never handed over
  3792  (3107)                  % as a computed-bounds subarray of cache - that subarray form
  3793  (3108)                  % painted line 0's bytes on every row while the cache held
  3794  (3109)                  % each line's own text. See the note above paintRow.
  3795  (3110)                  IF want < 0 THEN
  3796  (3111)                      FOR base IN 0:lineWide - 1 DO
  3797  (3112)                          blankRow(base) =: paintBuf(base)
  3798  (3113)                      ENDFOR
  3799  (3114)                  ELSE
  3800  (3115)                      slotAt(first + i) =: idx
  3801  (3116)                      idx * lineWide =: base
  3802  (3117)                      FOR idx IN 0:lineWide - 1 DO
  3803  (3118)                          cache(base + idx) =: paintBuf(idx)
  3804  (3119)                      ENDFOR
  3805  (3120)                  ENDIF
  3806  (3121)                  paintRow(firstMsgRow + i)
  3807  (3122)  
  3808  (3123)                  % THE SHADOW RECORDS THE LINE, NOT WHAT REACHED THE GLASS.
  3809  (3124)                  % A row that was partly covered is remembered as painted, which
  3810  (3125)                  % is right while the panel is up - and is exactly why
  3811  (3126)                  % closePanel must forget the rows the panel covered before it
  3812  (3127)                  % repaints. That is what makes the close cheap: only those rows
  3813  (3128)                  % are stale, so only those are drawn again.
  3814  (3129)                  want =: rowShown(i)
  3815  (3130)              ENDIF
  3816  (3131)          ENDFOR
  3817  (3132)          winLeave
  3818  (3133)  
  3819  (3134)          % SHOW IT. VTM holds its output in a buffer and a terminal READ is what
  3820  (3135)          % normally empties it, which is why every screen program that redraws
  3821  (3136)          % and then waits for a key appears to work without ever asking.
  3822  (3137)          %
  3823  (3138)          % This client does NOT wait for a key - it polls - so a repaint driven
  3824  (3139)          % by an arriving message has no read behind it and would sit in the
  3825  (3140)          % buffer. MEASURED on D100 2026-08-26: a line said in a freshly joined
  3826  (3141)          % room stayed invisible until any keystroke, which reads exactly like a
  3827  (3142)          % lost message and is nothing of the kind.
  3828  (3143)          %
  3829  (3144)          % The callers flush too. Flushing an empty buffer costs nothing, and a
  3830  (3145)          % painter that leaves its work invisible is the trap this file has now
  3831  (3146)          % paid for twice.
  3832  (3147)          VTWBUF =: st
  3833  (3148)      ENDROUTINE
  3834  (3149)  
  3835  (3150)      % =====================================================================
  3836  (3151)      % THE TOP SECTION
  3837  (3152)      % =====================================================================
  3838  (3153)  
  3839  (3154)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : drawStatus
  3840  (3155)          INTEGER : st
  3841  (3156)          INTEGER : at
  3842  (3157)          INTEGER : i
  3843  (3158)          INTEGER : nowView
  3844  (3159)          INTEGER : nowRemote
  3845  (3160)          INTEGER : same
  3846  (3161)          INTEGER : take        % how much of the topic fits in the room field
  3847  (3162)  
  3848  (3163)          MN113(cal)
  3849  (3164)  
  3850  (3165)          % NOTHING ON IT CHANGED? THEN DO NOT PAINT IT.
  3851  (3166)          %
  3852  (3167)          % Called on every arriving message, and its content moves only when
  3853  (3168)          % the clock ticks a minute, the count changes, the room changes or
  3854  (3169)          % scrollback is entered or left. The rest of the time this put back
  3855  (3170)          % exactly what was already there - eighty characters a message.
  3856  (3171)          %
  3857  (3172)          % The clock is compared to the MINUTE because that is all it shows.
  3858  (3173)          % Comparing seconds would make it repaint every second for a display
  3859  (3174)          % that cannot show the difference.
  3860  (3175)          0 =: nowView
  3861  (3176)          IF viewFrom > 0 THEN
  3862  (3177)              1 =: nowView
  3863  (3178)          ENDIF
  3864  (3179)          0 =: nowRemote
  3865  (3180)          IF sawRemote THEN
  3866  (3181)              1 =: nowRemote
  3867  (3182)          ENDIF
  3868  (3183)  
  3869  (3184)          1 =: same
  3870  (3185)          IF cal(2) >< lastMin THEN
  3871  (3186)              0 =: same
  3872  (3187)          ENDIF
  3873  (3188)          IF cal(3) >< lastHour THEN
  3874  (3189)              0 =: same
  3875  (3190)          ENDIF
  3876  (3191)          IF hereCount >< lastCount THEN
  3877  (3192)              0 =: same
  3878  (3193)          ENDIF
  3879  (3194)          IF nowView >< lastView THEN
  3880  (3195)              0 =: same
  3881  (3196)          ENDIF
  3882  (3197)          IF nowRemote >< lastRemote THEN
  3883  (3198)              0 =: same
  3884  (3199)          ENDIF
  3885  (3200)          IF lenRoomName >< lastRoomLen THEN
  3886  (3201)              0 =: same
  3887  (3202)          ENDIF
  3888  (3203)          IF lenTopic >< lastTopicL THEN
  3889  (3204)              0 =: same
  3890  (3205)          ENDIF
  3891  (3206)          IF same >< 0 THEN
  3892  (3207)              % The NAME as well as its length - two rooms can be the same
  3893  (3208)              % length, and a switch between them would otherwise leave the old
  3894  (3209)              % name on the line.
  3895  (3210)              IF lenRoomName > 0 THEN
  3896  (3211)                  FOR i IN 1:lenRoomName DO
  3897  (3212)                      IF i <= 20 THEN
  3898  (3213)                          IF roomName(i - 1) >< lastRoom(i - 1) THEN
  3899  (3214)                              0 =: same
  3900  (3215)                          ENDIF
  3901  (3216)                      ENDIF
  3902  (3217)                  ENDFOR
  3903  (3218)              ENDIF
  3904  (3219)          ENDIF
  3905  (3220)  
  3906  (3221)          IF same >< 0 THEN
  3907  (3222)              RETURN
  3908  (3223)          ENDIF
  3909  (3224)  
  3910  (3225)          cal(2) =: lastMin
  3911  (3226)          cal(3) =: lastHour
  3912  (3227)          hereCount =: lastCount
  3913  (3228)          nowView =: lastView
  3914  (3229)          nowRemote =: lastRemote
  3915  (3230)          lenRoomName =: lastRoomLen
  3916  (3231)          lenTopic =: lastTopicL
  3917  (3232)          IF lenRoomName > 0 THEN
  3918  (3233)              FOR i IN 1:lenRoomName DO
  3919  (3234)                  IF i <= 20 THEN
  3920  (3235)                      roomName(i - 1) =: lastRoom(i - 1)
  3921  (3236)                  ENDIF
  3922  (3237)              ENDFOR
  3923  (3238)          ENDIF
  3924  (3239)  
  3925  (3240)          winEnter(topRow, 1, topHigh, scrWide)
  3926  (3241)          VTCREC(2, 2, 2, scrWide - 1) =: st
  3927  (3242)  
  3928  (3243)          % ROW 2, laid out for the WORST CASE rather than for this room's name.
  3929  (3244)          % A room name may be sixteen characters and '@trunk' is six more, so
  3930  (3245)          % the name field can reach column 24 - which is why the count no longer
  3931  (3246)          % sits at 19. A layout measured against one example name is a layout
  3932  (3247)          % that breaks on the next one.
  3933  (3248)          %
  3934  (3249)          %   col  3..24   room name, and '@trunk' if it reaches another machine
  3935  (3250)          %   col 26..29   how many are here
  3936  (3251)          %   col 31..34   'here'
  3937  (3252)          %   col 37..58   the hint, OR the scrollback warning - never both
  3938  (3253)          %   col 64..74   the clock
  3939  (3254)          % THE WIDTH IS PASSED, NOT LEFT AT 0. Width 0 means "the string says its
  3940  (3255)          % own length", which is right for a LITERAL. For a SUBARRAY the two
  3941  (3256)          % measurements we have DISAGREE, and neither has been explained:
  3942  (3257)          % TESTUI's paintRun painted a subarray of a 700-byte buffer with 0 and
  3943  (3258)          % the line ran off the right-hand side, wiping two window borders; this
  3944  (3259)          % call paints roomName(0:lenRoomName-1) with 0 and the live screen shows
  3945  (3260)          % exactly five characters for LOBBY, which is correct. The difference may
  3946  (3261)          % be that this subarray starts at index 0 and TESTUI's at a computed
  3947  (3262)          % offset - UNVERIFIED, and not worth a build cycle to find out, because
  3948  (3263)          % passing lenRoomName is what width 0 is SUPPOSED to mean here. So it is
  3949  (3264)          % a no-op if 0 behaves and a fix if it does not.
  3950  (3265)          bytdis(2, 3, lenRoomName, roomName(0:lenRoomName - 1), '')
  3951  (3266)          % THE MARKER IS APPENDED TO THE NAME with no gap, as the design shows,
  3952  (3267)          % so it reads as part of the room's identity and not as another field.
  3953  (3268)          3 + lenRoomName =: at
  3954  (3269)          IF sawRemote THEN
  3955  (3270)              bytdis(2, at, 0, '@trunk', '')
  3956  (3271)              at + 6 =: at
  3957  (3272)          ENDIF
  3958  (3273)  
  3959  (3274)          % THE TOPIC, AS "LOBBY #the topic".
  3960  (3275)          %
  3961  (3276)          % It reads as part of the room's identity like '@trunk' does, which is
  3962  (3277)          % what Ronny asked for: the header said LOBBY and setting a topic
  3963  (3278)          % changed nothing.
  3964  (3279)          %
  3965  (3280)          % CLAMPED TO THE FIELD, WHICH IS NARROW. The count starts at column 26
  3966  (3281)          % and the room field must stop at 24 to leave a gap, so what is left for
  3967  (3282)          % a topic is 24 minus wherever the name ended, minus the two characters
  3968  (3283)          % of ' #'. With a five-character room name and no trunk marker that is
  3969  (3284)          % about fourteen characters; with '@trunk' up there too it is about
  3970  (3285)          % eight, and a long room name can leave none at all - in which case
  3971  (3286)          % nothing is drawn rather than something ragged running into the count.
  3972  (3287)          %
  3973  (3288)          % THE FULL TOPIC IS STILL ONE COMMAND AWAY - /topic prints it in a
  3974  (3289)          % window with no width problem. This line is a reminder, not the
  3975  (3290)          % record. Widening it means moving the count, the 'here' and the hint,
  3976  (3291)          % which is a layout decision and not this fix.
  3977  (3292)          IF lenTopic > 0 THEN
  3978  (3293)              24 - at - 1 =: take
  3979  (3294)              IF take > lenTopic THEN
  3980  (3295)                  lenTopic =: take
  3981  (3296)              ENDIF
  3982  (3297)              IF take > 0 THEN
  3983  (3298)                  bytdis(2, at, 0, ' #', '')
  3984  (3299)                  bytdis(2, at + 2, take, curTopic(0 : take - 1), '')
  3985  (3300)              ENDIF
  3986  (3301)          ENDIF
  3987  (3302)          intdis(2, 26, 4, hereCount, '')
  3988  (3303)          bytdis(2, 31, 0, 'here', '')
  3989  (3304)  
  3990  (3305)          % The clock, into lineBuf because put2 works there.
  3991  (3306)          blankLine
  3992  (3307)          put2(0, cal(4))                    % day
  3993  (3308)          spaceByte(0) =: lineBuf(2)
  3994  (3309)          put2(3, cal(5))                    % month
  3995  (3310)          spaceByte(0) =: lineBuf(5)
  3996  (3311)          put2(6, cal(3))                    % hour
  3997  (3312)          colonByte(0) =: lineBuf(8)
  3998  (3313)          put2(9, cal(2))                    % minute
  3999  (3314)          bytdis(2, 64, 11, lineBuf(0:10), '')
  4000  (3315)  
  4001  (3316)          % PAGED BACK? Say so, because the arrival rule above means the screen
  4002  (3317)          % deliberately does not follow new lines while the user is reading.
  4003  (3318)          % Without this the client looks stuck.
  4004  (3319)          % THE HINT AND THE WARNING SHARE ONE SLOT, and that is deliberate.
  4005  (3320)          %
  4006  (3321)          % They used to sit side by side and the warning printed straight
  4007  (3322)          % THROUGH the hint - 'Use /help to lSCROLLBACKds' - which was patched
  4008  (3323)          % by moving it along until it fitted. Two things that are never both
  4009  (3324)          % worth reading should not both be drawn: the hint matters when nothing
  4010  (3325)          % is happening, and the moment the user has paged back, where they are
  4011  (3326)          % matters more than where the help is.
  4012  (3327)          IF viewFrom > 0 THEN
  4013  (3328)              bytdis(2, 37, 0, 'SCROLLBACK - PAGE DOWN ', '')
  4014  (3329)          ELSE
  4015  (3330)              bytdis(2, 37, 0, '/help for commands     ', '')
  4016  (3331)          ENDIF
  4017  (3332)          winLeave
  4018  (3333)      ENDROUTINE
  4019  (3334)  
  4020  (3335)      % =====================================================================
  4021  (3336)      % THE BOTTOM SECTION
  4022  (3337)      % =====================================================================
  4023  (3338)  
  4024  (3339)      % Repaint the input line and leave the cursor at the end of it.
  4025  (3340)      %
  4026  (3341)      % THE CURSOR BELONGS HERE. In TESTUI it is parked somewhere harmless
  4027  (3342)      % because stray echoes landed inside windows; here there IS a right place
  4028  (3343)      % for it, so every repaint of anything must finish by calling this.
  4029  (3344)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : drawInput
  4030  (3345)          INTEGER : st
  4031  (3346)  
  4032  (3347)          winEnter(botRow, 1, botHigh, scrWide)
  4033  (3348)  
  4034  (3349)          % THE CURSOR IS PUT BACK WHATEVER HAPPENS, because SINTRAN echoes a
  4035  (3350)          % typed character wherever the cursor was left and every repaint above
  4036  (3351)          % moves it. That is a few bytes.
  4037  (3352)          %
  4038  (3353)          % THE ROW ITSELF IS ONLY REDRAWN WHEN IT CHANGED. It changes when
  4039  (3354)          % somebody types, and not when a message arrives - and it was being
  4040  (3355)          % cleared and rewritten on every single arrival.
  4041  (3356)          IF inputDirty THEN
  4042  (3357)              VTCREC(inpRow, 2, inpRow, scrWide - 1) =: st
  4043  (3358)              bytdis(inpRow, 2, 0, '>', '')
  4044  (3359)              IF typedLen > 0 THEN
  4045  (3360)                  bytdis(inpRow, inpCol, typedLen, typed(0:typedLen - 1), '')
  4046  (3361)              ENDIF
  4047  (3362)              FALSE =: inputDirty
  4048  (3363)          ENDIF
  4049  (3364)  
  4050  (3365)          VTPCUR(inpRow, inpCol + typedLen) =: st
  4051  (3366)          winLeave
  4052  (3367)      ENDROUTINE
  4053  (3368)  
  4054  (3369)      % THE WINDOW BAR - every window that exists, and which one you are in.
  4055  (3370)      %
  4056  (3371)      % It once held a literal lifted straight out of the mockup: five room
  4057  (3372)      % names and two people who did not exist. That was worse than empty,
  4058  (3373)      % because a screen full of invented state looks finished when it is not.
  4059  (3374)      % Everything on it now is read from the window table.
  4060  (3375)      %
  4061  (3376)      % Window 1 is the room. Window 2 upward are conversations and are drawn
  4062  (3377)      % with a leading = , which is what tells a person called LOBBY apart from
  4063  (3378)      % a room called LOBBY at a glance.
  4064  (3379)      %
  4065  (3380)      % THE CURRENT ONE IS IN BRACKETS rather than marked with a colour or a
  4066  (3381)      % highlight. This terminal has attributes, but a bracket survives a
  4067  (3382)      % screen-grab, a printer and a terminal that ignores them - and the whole
  4068  (3383)      % bar has to stay readable on the worst terminal in the room.
  4069  (3384)      %
  4070  (3385)      %   [1 LOBBY]  2 =KARI 3*  4 =OLAV 12
  4071  (3386)      %
  4072  (3387)      % A number after a window is UNREAD LINES. A * means your own nick was
  4073  (3388)      % said in it, which is the one thing worth interrupting for, so it is not
  4074  (3389)      % folded into the count - a person scanning the bar looks for the star
  4075  (3390)      % first and reads the numbers second.
  4076  (3391)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : drawBar
  4077  (3392)          INTEGER : at
  4078  (3393)          INTEGER : w
  4079  (3394)          INTEGER : i
  4080  (3395)          INTEGER : st
  4081  (3396)  
  4082  (3397)          winEnter(botRow, 1, botHigh, scrWide)
  4083  (3398)          VTCREC(3, 2, 3, scrWide - 1) =: st
  4084  (3399)  
  4085  (3400)          blankLine
  4086  (3401)          0 =: at
  4087  (3402)          FOR w IN 1:maxWins DO
  4088  (3403)              IF winUsed(w) >< 0 THEN
  4089  (3404)  
  4090  (3405)                  % CLAMPED AGAINST THE LINE BUFFER, not against a guess at how
  4091  (3406)                  % many windows fit. Four windows with sixteen-character names
  4092  (3407)                  % and four-digit counts would overrun 77 columns, and PLANC
  4093  (3408)                  % checks no bound - the write would simply carry on into
  4094  (3409)                  % whatever follows lineBuf.
  4095  (3410)                  IF at < lineWide - 24 THEN
  4096  (3411)                      IF w = curWin THEN
  4097  (3412)                          at + putField(at, 1, '[') =: at
  4098  (3413)                      ELSE
  4099  (3414)                          IF at > 0 THEN
  4100  (3415)                              at + putField(at, 1, ' ') =: at
  4101  (3416)                          ENDIF
  4102  (3417)                      ENDIF
  4103  (3418)  
  4104  (3419)                      digits(w) =: lineBuf(at)
  4105  (3420)                      at + 1 =: at
  4106  (3421)                      at + putField(at, 1, ' ') =: at
  4107  (3422)  
  4108  (3423)                      IF w = 1 THEN
  4109  (3424)                          IF joined THEN
  4110  (3425)                              at + putField(at, lenRoomName, &
  4111  (3426)                                  roomName(0:lenRoomName - 1)) =: at
  4112  (3427)                          ELSE
  4113  (3428)                              % NOT IN A ROOM IS A REAL STATE and it says so,
  4114  (3429)                              % rather than leaving a bare number with nothing
  4115  (3430)                              % after it.
  4116  (3431)                              at + putField(at, 11, 'no room yet') =: at
  4117  (3432)                          ENDIF
  4118  (3433)                      ELSE
  4119  (3434)                          at + putField(at, 1, '=') =: at
  4120  (3435)  
  4121  (3436)                          % COPIED BYTE BY BYTE RATHER THAN THROUGH putField.
  4122  (3437)                          %
  4123  (3438)                          % The putField form was
  4124  (3439)                          %     at + putField(at, winLabLen(w), &
  4125  (3440)                          %         winLabel(w * 16 : w * 16 + winLabLen(w) - 1)) &
  4126  (3441)                          %         =: at
  4127  (3442)                          % and it printed NOTHING while the label was plainly
  4128  (3443)                          % there - a temporary diagnostic on this very line
  4129  (3444)                          % showed winLabLen reading 9 for a stored "FJELL!BOB".
  4130  (3445)                          % Three readings of the source did not explain it; the
  4131  (3446)                          % measurement took one build.
  4132  (3447)                          %
  4133  (3448)                          % A subarray whose bounds are both computed from an
  4134  (3449)                          % expression is the one thing that call did which
  4135  (3450)                          % nothing else in this file does, and an explicit loop
  4136  (3451)                          % removes the question entirely. It is also no slower:
  4137  (3452)                          % putField copies a byte at a time too.
  4138  (3453)                          IF winLabLen(w) > 0 THEN
  4139  (3454)                              FOR i IN 1:winLabLen(w) DO
  4140  (3455)                                  winLabel(w * 16 + i - 1) =: lineBuf(at)
  4141  (3456)                                  at + 1 =: at
  4142  (3457)                              ENDFOR
  4143  (3458)                          ENDIF
  4144  (3459)                      ENDIF
  4145  (3460)  
  4146  (3461)                      % THE STAR FIRST, THEN THE COUNT - see the note above.
  4147  (3462)                      IF winBell(w) >< 0 THEN
  4148  (3463)                          at + putField(at, 1, '*') =: at
  4149  (3464)                      ENDIF
  4150  (3465)                      IF winUnread(w) > 0 THEN
  4151  (3466)                          at + putField(at, 1, ' ') =: at
  4152  (3467)                          at + putNum(at, winUnread(w)) =: at
  4153  (3468)                      ENDIF
  4154  (3469)  
  4155  (3470)                      IF w = curWin THEN
  4156  (3471)                          at + putField(at, 1, ']') =: at
  4157  (3472)                      ENDIF
  4158  (3473)                  ENDIF
  4159  (3474)              ENDIF
  4160  (3475)          ENDFOR
  4161  (3476)  
  4162  (3477)          IF at > 0 THEN
  4163  (3478)              bytdis(3, 3, at, lineBuf(0:at - 1), '')
  4164  (3479)          ENDIF
  4165  (3480)          winLeave
  4166  (3481)      ENDROUTINE
  4167  (3482)  
  4168  (3483)      % =====================================================================
  4169  (3484)      % THE WHOLE SCREEN - drawn ONCE
  4170  (3485)      % =====================================================================
  4171  (3486)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : drawAll
  4172  (3487)          INTEGER : at
  4173  (3488)          INTEGER : col
  4174  (3489)  
  4175  (3490)          % ONE BOX ROUND THE WHOLE SCREEN, then a rule across it for each
  4176  (3491)          % separator.
  4177  (3492)          %
  4178  (3493)          % THREE STACKED FRAMES WAS THE FIRST ATTEMPT AND IT LOOKED WRONG.
  4179  (3494)          % frame() draws a box, so where two of them touch you get the TOP
  4180  (3495)          % CORNERS of the lower box - a fresh box top sitting inside the
  4181  (3496)          % outer one - where the design wants a cross-piece. PLANC-SCREEN-H
  4182  (3497)          % has no T-junction verb, so the corners cannot be had that way.
  4183  (3498)          %
  4184  (3499)          % fullbar is the library's own horizontal rule and is what the
  4185  (3500)          % separators are for.
  4186  (3501)          winEnter(1, 1, 24, scrWide)
  4187  (3502)          frame(1, 1, 24, scrWide, 'SPACE-FILL')
  4188  (3503)          fullbar(midRow, 2, 1, scrWide - 2)
  4189  (3504)          fullbar(botRow, 2, 1, scrWide - 2)
  4190  (3505)          winLeave
  4191  (3506)  
  4192  (3507)          % The title is PAINTED OVER the top frame's own edge, which is what
  4193  (3508)          % the mockup shows. frame's fifth argument is ATTRIBUTES, not a title.
  4194  (3509)          bytdis(1, 4, 0, ' NDCHAT 2.1 ', '')
  4195  (3510)          % NAME@MACHINE - and the machine is the one the SERVER named in the
  4196  (3511)          % welcome, never one this client made up.
  4197  (3512)          %
  4198  (3513)          % CHATUI drew ARNE@D100 from two literals. This draws ARNE@FJELL, or
  4199  (3514)          % ARNE@D100 on a machine that has not been given a name, or ARNE alone
  4200  (3515)          % if the server never said - which is what an older server does, and
  4201  (3516)          % showing the name alone is the honest answer to not being told.
  4202  (3517)          blankLine
  4203  (3518)          0 =: at
  4204  (3519)          at + putField(at, widWho, myName(0:lenMyName - 1)) =: at
  4205  (3520)          IF lenMyMach > 0 THEN
  4206  (3521)              atByte(0) =: lineBuf(at)
  4207  (3522)              at + 1 =: at
  4208  (3523)              at + putField(at, 16, myMachine(0:lenMyMach - 1)) =: at
  4209  (3524)          ENDIF
  4210  (3525)          % RIGHT-ALIGNED so it always ENDS at column 77, just inside the frame.
  4211  (3526)          % A fixed start column was fine when this was the literal ARNE@D100 and
  4212  (3527)          % nine characters wide. A machine may now be called anything up to
  4213  (3528)          % sixteen, so a fixed start would run the corner straight through the
  4214  (3529)          % right-hand border on the first long name somebody chose.
  4215  (3530)          78 - at =: col
  4216  (3531)          IF col < 20 THEN
  4217  (3532)              20 =: col
  4218  (3533)          ENDIF
  4219  (3534)          bytdis(1, col, at, lineBuf(0:at - 1), '')
  4220  (3535)  
  4221  (3536)          % A FULL REPAINT STARTS FROM NOTHING KNOWN. drawAll is only ever
  4222  (3537)          % called when the whole screen has to be rebuilt, so anything the
  4223  (3538)          % shadow believed about the pane is stale by definition.
  4224  (3539)          0 =: shadowWin
  4225  (3540)          TRUE =: inputDirty
  4226  (3541)  
  4227  (3542)          % AND NOTHING IS ON TOP ANY MORE. drawAll has just painted over the
  4228  (3543)          % whole screen, panel included, so leaving panelUp set would say a
  4229  (3544)          % window is up that the user cannot see: the next keypress would be
  4230  (3545)          % swallowed "closing" it, and closePanel would then repaint a rectangle
  4231  (3546)          % that already holds the room. Two paths reach here with a panel
  4232  (3547)          % possibly open - the welcome, which redraws the frame once a session
  4233  (3548)          % when the machine's name arrives, and a room change.
  4234  (3549)          FALSE =: panelUp
  4235  (3550)  
  4236  (3551)          % And the status line has just had its row wiped by the frame, so it
  4237  (3552)          % must paint whatever it last showed.
  4238  (3553)          0 - 1 =: lastMin
  4239  (3554)  
  4240  (3555)          drawStatus
  4241  (3556)          paintChat
  4242  (3557)          drawBar
  4243  (3558)          drawInput
  4244  (3559)      ENDROUTINE
  4245  (3560)  
  4246  (3561)      % =====================================================================
  4247  (3562)      % WHICH WINDOW BELONGS TO A PERSON
  4248  (3563)      %
  4249  (3564)      % Answers an existing one if there is one, opens the lowest free one if
  4250  (3565)      % not, and answers ZERO when every window is taken. Zero is a real answer
  4251  (3566)      % and the callers deal with it by putting the line in the room instead -
  4252  (3567)      % losing a private message because the bar was full would be the worst
  4253  (3568)      % possible way to run out of a resource.
  4254  (3569)      %
  4255  (3570)      % THE COMPARISON IS EXACT, including case. Every name in this system comes
  4256  (3571)      % from the server, which has already settled what a name looks like; a
  4257  (3572)      % client that matched loosely would open a second window for the same
  4258  (3573)      % person the first time somebody typed a name differently.
  4259  (3574)      % =====================================================================
  4260  (3575)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : winFor(at, n)
  4261  (3576)          INTEGER : w
  4262  (3577)          INTEGER : i
  4263  (3578)          INTEGER : same
  4264  (3579)          INTEGER : take
  4265  (3580)  
  4266  (3581)          n =: take
  4267  (3582)          IF take > 16 THEN
  4268  (3583)              16 =: take
  4269  (3584)          ENDIF
  4270  (3585)          IF take < 1 THEN
  4271  (3586)              0 RETURN
  4272  (3587)          ENDIF
  4273  (3588)  
  4274  (3589)          % An existing conversation. Window 1 is the room and is never a match.
  4275  (3590)          FOR w IN 2:maxWins DO
  4276  (3591)              IF winUsed(w) >< 0 THEN
  4277  (3592)                  IF winLabLen(w) = take THEN
  4278  (3593)                      1 =: same
  4279  (3594)                      FOR i IN 1:take DO
  4280  (3595)                          IF winLabel(w * 16 + i - 1) >< inBuf(at + i - 1) THEN
  4281  (3596)                              0 =: same
  4282  (3597)                          ENDIF
  4283  (3598)                      ENDFOR
  4284  (3599)                      IF same >< 0 THEN
  4285  (3600)                          w RETURN
  4286  (3601)                      ENDIF
  4287  (3602)                  ENDIF
  4288  (3603)              ENDIF
  4289  (3604)          ENDFOR
  4290  (3605)  
  4291  (3606)          % A new one. Opened EMPTY rather than seeded with anything: the line
  4292  (3607)          % that caused it to open is added by the caller, in the ordinary way,
  4293  (3608)          % so there is only one path that ever puts a line in a window.
  4294  (3609)          FOR w IN 2:maxWins DO
  4295  (3610)              IF winUsed(w) = 0 THEN
  4296  (3611)                  1 =: winUsed(w)
  4297  (3612)                  0 =: winCount(w)
  4298  (3613)                  0 =: winFirst(w)
  4299  (3614)                  0 =: winView(w)
  4300  (3615)                  0 =: winUnread(w)
  4301  (3616)                  0 =: winBell(w)
  4302  (3617)                  winName(w, at, take)
  4303  (3618)                  w RETURN
  4304  (3619)              ENDIF
  4305  (3620)          ENDFOR
  4306  (3621)  
  4307  (3622)          0 RETURN
  4308  (3623)      ENDROUTINE
  4309  (3624)  
  4310  (3625)      % =====================================================================
  4311  (3626)      % SWITCHING WINDOWS
  4312  (3627)      %
  4313  (3628)      % The whole screen is repainted, not just the middle. The status line
  4314  (3629)      % carries the room name and the bar carries the brackets, so painting only
  4315  (3630)      % the middle would leave both of them describing the window you just left -
  4316  (3631)      % and a header that disagrees with the text under it is worse than a slow
  4317  (3632)      % repaint.
  4318  (3633)      %
  4319  (3634)      % A WINDOW THAT DOES NOT EXIST IS NOT AN ERROR. Typing /w 3 with three
  4320  (3635)      % windows open is a normal thing to do while the third is being closed by
  4321  (3636)      % somebody else, and refusing it loudly would put a notice in the room
  4322  (3637)      % over a keystroke nobody meant anything by.
  4323  (3638)      % =====================================================================
  4324  (3639)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER) : winGo(w)
  4325  (3640)          IF w < 1 THEN
  4326  (3641)              RETURN
  4327  (3642)          ENDIF
  4328  (3643)          IF w > maxWins THEN
  4329  (3644)              RETURN
  4330  (3645)          ENDIF
  4331  (3646)          IF winUsed(w) = 0 THEN
  4332  (3647)              RETURN
  4333  (3648)          ENDIF
  4334  (3649)          IF w = curWin THEN
  4335  (3650)              RETURN
  4336  (3651)          ENDIF
  4337  (3652)  
  4338  (3653)          % SAVE BEFORE LOAD, always. The two are a pair - see winSave.
  4339  (3654)          winSave
  4340  (3655)          winUse(w)
  4341  (3656)  
  4342  (3657)          % LOOKED AT, so it is no longer unread and no longer ringing. This is
  4343  (3658)          % the one path where that is true - see winUse.
  4344  (3659)          0 =: winUnread(w)
  4345  (3660)          0 =: winBell(w)
  4346  (3661)  
  4347  (3662)          % ONLY WHAT CHANGED. Switching windows changes the pane, the status
  4348  (3663)          % line and the bar - and nothing else. The frame, the title and the
  4349  (3664)          % typed line are all exactly as they were, so drawAll would rub out
  4350  (3665)          % twenty-four rows to alter three regions.
  4351  (3666)          %
  4352  (3667)          % This is TESTUI's rule and it is the one this file got wrong: put
  4353  (3668)          % back what the change actually touched, never the whole screen.
  4354  (3669)          IF screenOn THEN
  4355  (3670)              paintChat
  4356  (3671)              drawStatus
  4357  (3672)              drawBar
  4358  (3673)              drawInput
  4359  (3674)              flush
  4360  (3675)          ENDIF
  4361  (3676)      ENDROUTINE
  4362  (3677)  
  4363  (3678)      % The next window that exists, wrapping round. This is what a single key
  4364  (3679)      % gets you: with two windows open it is a toggle, which is the case that
  4365  (3680)      % matters most - one room and one person.
  4366  (3681)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : winNext
  4367  (3682)          INTEGER : i
  4368  (3683)          INTEGER : w
  4369  (3684)  
  4370  (3685)          FOR i IN 1:maxWins DO
  4371  (3686)              curWin + i =: w
  4372  (3687)              DO
  4373  (3688)                  WHILE w > maxWins
  4374  (3689)                  w - maxWins =: w
  4375  (3690)              ENDDO
  4376  (3691)              IF winUsed(w) >< 0 THEN
  4377  (3692)                  winGo(w)
  4378  (3693)                  RETURN
  4379  (3694)              ENDIF
  4380  (3695)          ENDFOR
  4381  (3696)      ENDROUTINE
  4382  (3697)  
  4383  (3698)      % =====================================================================
  4384  (3699)      % THE FAKE FEED - one made-up line
  4385  (3700)      % =====================================================================
  4386  (3701)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : pageUp
  4387  (3702)          INTEGER : most
  4388  (3703)          cacheCount - msgRows =: most
  4389  (3704)          IF most < 0 THEN
  4390  (3705)              0 =: most
  4391  (3706)          ENDIF
  4392  (3707)          viewFrom + msgRows =: viewFrom
  4393  (3708)          IF viewFrom > most THEN
  4394  (3709)              most =: viewFrom
  4395  (3710)          ENDIF
  4396  (3711)          paintChat
  4397  (3712)          drawStatus
  4398  (3713)          drawInput
  4399  (3714)          flush
  4400  (3715)      ENDROUTINE
  4401  (3716)  
  4402  (3717)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : pageDown
  4403  (3718)          viewFrom - msgRows =: viewFrom
  4404  (3719)          IF viewFrom < 0 THEN
  4405  (3720)              0 =: viewFrom
  4406  (3721)          ENDIF
  4407  (3722)          paintChat
  4408  (3723)          drawStatus
  4409  (3724)          drawInput
  4410  (3725)          flush
  4411  (3726)      ENDROUTINE
  4412  (3727)  
  4413  (3728)      % =====================================================================
  4414  (3729)      % THE KEYBOARD
  4415  (3730)      % =====================================================================
  4416  (3731)
