  5766  (5081)          % have gone quiet and told nobody. Two different failures from one
  5767  (5082)          % cause, which is why both programs needed their own answer.
  5768  (5083)          %
  5769  (5084)          % Stopping is the honest one here. There is no reconnect to attempt: a
  5770  (5085)          % port belongs to an XMSG that is gone, and when it comes back this
  5771  (5086)          % program has to open a new one and rejoin from the beginning - which
  5772  (5087)          % is exactly what starting it again does.
  5773  (5088)          IF returnStatus = XMXENRU THEN
  5774  (5089)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL27', 'XMSG has stopped - leaving$')
  5775  (5090)              FALSE =: joined
  5776  (5091)              0 =: serverMagic
  5777  (5092)              FALSE =: running
  5778  (5093)          ENDIF
  5779  (5094)      ENDROUTINE
  5780  (5095)  
  5781  (5096)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5782  (5097)      % Does the typed line begin with "/word"? Case is folded, so /WHO and /who
  5783  (5098)      % are the same command.
  5784  (5099)      %
  5785  (5100)      % typed(0) is the slash, so the word starts at 1. A match also requires the
  5786  (5101)      % character AFTER the word to be a space or the end of the line - without
  5787  (5102)      % that test "/nickname" would match "/nick" and the rest be read as an
  5788  (5103)      % argument.
  5789  (5104)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5790  (5105)      % THE LENGTH IS NOT A PARAMETER - THE KEYWORD IS ASKED FOR IT.
  5791  (5106)      %
  5792  (5107)      % Every caller passes a literal, and each one used to hand the count in
  5793  (5108)      % beside it. Rename a command, or add one, and the number beside it is
  5794  (5109)      % silently wrong: the build is clean and the match then runs over the wrong
  5795  (5110)      % number of characters, so /nick could start matching /nickname or stop
  5796  (5111)      % matching at all. The same trap as an 'ALn' field width.
  5797  (5112)      %
  5798  (5113)      % MAXINDEX(word, 1) is the DECLARED UPPER BOUND and a string literal
  5799  (5114)      % subscripts from ZERO, so the length is that plus one.
  5800  (5115)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (BYTES) : cmdIs(word)
  5801  (5116)          INTEGER : i, c, w, wordLen
  5802  (5117)          MAXINDEX(word, 1) + 1 =: wordLen
  5803  (5118)  
  5804  (5119)          IF typedLen < wordLen + 1 THEN
  5805  (5120)              0 RETURN
  5806  (5121)          ENDIF
  5807  (5122)  
  5808  (5123)          FOR i IN 1:wordLen DO
  5809  (5124)              typed(i) =: c
  5810  (5125)              % Fold lower case to upper: 'a' is 97, 'z' is 122.
  5811  (5126)              IF c >= 97 AND c <= 122 THEN
  5812  (5127)                  c - 32 =: c
  5813  (5128)              ENDIF
  5814  (5129)              word(i - 1) =: w
  5815  (5130)              IF c >< w THEN
  5816  (5131)                  0 RETURN
  5817  (5132)              ENDIF
  5818  (5133)          ENDFOR
  5819  (5134)  
  5820  (5135)          % The word must END here - the next character is a space or nothing.
  5821  (5136)          IF typedLen = wordLen + 1 THEN
  5822  (5137)              1 RETURN
  5823  (5138)          ENDIF
  5824  (5139)  
  5825  (5140)          IF typed(wordLen + 1) = 32 THEN
  5826  (5141)              1 RETURN
  5827  (5142)          ENDIF
  5828  (5143)  
  5829  (5144)          0 RETURN
  5830  (5145)      ENDROUTINE
  5831  (5146)  
  5832  (5147)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5833  (5148)      % A line beginning with "/" is a command, not something to say.
  5834  (5149)      %
  5835  (5150)      % WHAT IS AND IS NOT IMPLEMENTED, and the difference matters. /nick, /leave
  5836  (5151)      % and /quit map onto message kinds that ALREADY EXIST on the wire - kRename
  5837  (5152)      % and kLeave - and /join re-sends the join CHAT already sends at startup, to
  5838  (5153)      % a different room name. None of those invent anything.
  5839  (5154)      %
  5840  (5155)      % /who does NOT map onto anything. There is no query kind in the format, and
  5841  (5156)      % the format is pinned byte-for-byte by ChatMessageGoldenBytesTests on the
  5842  (5157)      % C# side, so adding one is a change to BOTH ends in the same commit - not
  5843  (5158)      % something this file may do on its own. It says so rather than pretending.
  5844  (5159)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5845  (5160)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : handleCommand
  5846  (5161)          INTEGER : at, argLen, i, length
  5847  (5162)          % Where the name ends and the message begins in a /tell.
  5848  (5163)          INTEGER : cut
  5849  (5164)  
  5850  (5165)          IF cmdIs('HELP') = 1 THEN
  5851  (5166)              % ON A SCREEN THE HELP IS A WINDOW, and it is built from the same
  5852  (5167)              % table the line renderer prints below - so the two can never say
  5853  (5168)              % different things, and the window is measured from the text
  5854  (5169)              % rather than sized by a number somebody has to remember to update.
  5855  (5170)              IF screenOn THEN
  5856  (5171)                  buildHelp
  5857  (5172)                  drawPanel
  5858  (5173)                  flush
  5859  (5174)                  RETURN
  5860  (5175)              ENDIF
  5861  (5176)  
  5862  (5177)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL15', 'CHAT commands:$')
  5863  (5178)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL11', 'you are in ')
  5864  (5179)              showRoom
  5865  (5180)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '$')
  5866  (5181)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL47', '  /join <room>   join another room, short name$')
  5867  (5182)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL38', '  /nick <name>   change your nickname$')
  5868  (5183)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL37', '  /who           who is in this room$')
  5869  (5184)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL45', '  /list          rooms, and how many in each$')
  5870  (5185)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL47', '  /map           the machines and their trunks$')
  5871  (5186)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL37', '  /topic         show the room topic$')
  5872  (5187)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL24', '  /topic <text>  set it$')
  5873  (5188)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL', '  /tell <name> <text>  a message to one person$')
  5874  (5189)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL32', '  /leave         leave the room$')
  5875  (5190)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL', '  /exit          Exit chat program$')
  5876  (5191)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL36', '  anything else is said to the room$')
  5877  (5192)              RETURN
  5878  (5193)          ENDIF
  5879  (5194)  
  5880  (5195)          % /who - ask the room for its membership list.
  5881  (5196)          %
  5882  (5197)          % This used to print three lines saying the format had no kind for it.
  5883  (5198)          % It has one now: kWho = 11, added to ChatMessageKind.cs, CHATSV.PLNC
  5884  (5199)          % and here together, which is what those lines said the change needed.
  5885  (5200)          % The answer arrives later as an ordinary message and is printed by
  5886  (5201)          % showArrived - nothing waits here.
  5887  (5202)          % /who - who is in my room, or, WITH NO ROOM, who is anywhere.
  5888  (5203)          %
  5889  (5204)          % SENT EITHER WAY, and the same kind either way. This used to refuse
  5890  (5205)          % with "not in a room yet - wait or /join", which was the client
  5891  (5206)          % answering a question it is not the one that knows. The SERVER knows
  5892  (5207)          % whether we hold a seat, so it picks: kWho back if we do, kAllWho -
  5893  (5208)          % every room and everyone in it - if we do not.
  5894  (5209)          %
  5895  (5210)          % Ronny asked for exactly that on 2026-08-22, and it is the right shape
  5896  (5211)          % for the same reason /list is allowed unjoined: somebody with no room
  5897  (5212)          % is precisely the person who needs to be told where everybody is.
  5898  (5213)          %
  5899  (5214)          % THIS NEEDS serverMagic TO SURVIVE LEAVING, and it did not until the
  5900  (5215)          % same day. /leave used to zero it along with the joined flag, so this
  5901  (5216)          % question had no address to go to and printed nothing at all - the
  5902  (5217)          % server was answering correctly and the letter never left. The address
  5903  (5218)          % of the server and whether we hold a seat are two different facts; see
  5904  (5219)          % the note in the LEAVE branch.
  5905  (5220)          % ASKED BY HAND, so the answer is for this person and belongs in a
  5906  (5221)          % window rather than in everybody's room.
  5907  (5222)          IF cmdIs('WHO') = 1 THEN
  5908  (5223)              TRUE =: askedWho
  5909  (5224)              buildOwn(kWho, 0) =: length
  5910  (5225)              sendToServer(length)
  5911  (5226)              RETURN
  5912  (5227)          ENDIF
  5913  (5228)  
  5914  (5229)          % /list - which rooms exist and how many are in each.
  5915  (5230)          %
  5916  (5231)          % ALLOWED WHILE UNJOINED, unlike /who. "What rooms are there" is exactly
  5917  (5232)          % the question somebody asks when they have just been refused a name and
  5918  (5233)          % are deciding where to go, and refusing to answer it then would be
  5919  (5234)          % refusing at the one moment it is useful. The server needs no seat to
  5920  (5235)          % answer - it walks the seats it already has.
  5921  (5236)          IF cmdIs('LIST') = 1 THEN
  5922  (5237)              buildOwn(kRooms, 0) =: length
  5923  (5238)              sendToServer(length)
  5924  (5239)              RETURN
  5925  (5240)          ENDIF
  5926  (5241)  
  5927  (5242)          % /map - which machines there are and which trunks are up.
  5928  (5243)          %
  5929  (5244)          % ALLOWED WHILE UNJOINED, same as /list. "Is the other machine even
  5930  (5245)          % there?" is exactly the question somebody asks when the room looks
  5931  (5246)          % empty, and that is often when they have no seat either.
  5932  (5247)          %
  5933  (5248)          % READ-ONLY. There is deliberately no way to change a trunk from here -
  5934  (5249)          % START-TRUNK and STOP-TRUNK live in CHAT-MON, on the admin port, and
  5935  (5250)          % that separation is the whole reason /map is a room kind.
  5936  (5251)          IF cmdIs('MAP') = 1 THEN
  5937  (5252)              buildOwn(kMap, 0) =: length
  5938  (5253)              sendToServer(length)
  5939  (5254)              RETURN
  5940  (5255)          ENDIF
  5941  (5256)  
  5942  (5257)          % /topic          show this room's topic
  5943  (5258)          % /topic <text>   set it, and tell everybody in the room
  5944  (5259)          %
  5945  (5260)          % Needs a room, because a topic belongs to one. The server refuses a
  5946  (5261)          % stranger in silence, so asking while unjoined would simply do nothing
  5947  (5262)          % at all - say why instead.
  5948  (5263)          IF cmdIs('TOPIC') = 1 THEN
  5949  (5264)              IF NOT joined THEN
  5950  (5265)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL34', 'not in a room yet - wait or /join$')
  5951  (5266)                  RETURN
  5952  (5267)              ENDIF
  5953  (5268)  
  5954  (5269)              % 7 = past "/TOPIC ". If the line is exactly "/TOPIC" there is no
  5955  (5270)              % space and typedLen is 6, so argLen comes out negative - hence
  5956  (5271)              % the "< 1" test rather than "= 0".
  5957  (5272)              7 =: at
  5958  (5273)              typedLen - at =: argLen
  5959  (5274)              IF argLen < 1 THEN
  5960  (5275)                  % ASKING, not setting. An empty text is the question - and
  5961  (5276)                  % remembering that we asked is the only way to tell OUR answer
  5962  (5277)                  % from a topic somebody else changed, because both arrive as
  5963  (5278)                  % the same kind.
  5964  (5279)                  TRUE =: askedTopic
  5965  (5280)                  buildOwn(kTopic, 0) =: length
  5966  (5281)                  sendToServer(length)
  5967  (5282)                  RETURN
  5968  (5283)              ENDIF
  5969  (5284)  
  5970  (5285)              % Bounded to what the server will store, and said out loud rather
  5971  (5286)              % than quietly clipped - somebody who typed a long topic should not
  5972  (5287)              % have to work out for themselves where it was cut.
  5973  (5288)              IF argLen > maxTopicLen THEN
  5974  (5289)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL32', 'that topic is too long (max 64)$')
  5975  (5290)                  RETURN
  5976  (5291)              ENDIF
  5977  (5292)  
  5978  (5293)              buildOwnFrom(kTopic, at, argLen) =: length
  5979  (5294)              sendToServer(length)
  5980  (5295)              RETURN
  5981  (5296)          ENDIF
  5982  (5297)  
  5983  (5298)          IF cmdIs('JOIN') = 1 THEN
  5984  (5299)              6 =: at
  5985  (5300)              typedLen - at =: argLen
  5986  (5301)              IF argLen < 1 THEN
  5987  (5302)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL24', '/join needs a room name$')
  5988  (5303)                  RETURN
  5989  (5304)              ENDIF
  5990  (5305)              IF argLen > maxNameLen THEN
  5991  (5306)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL27', 'that room name is too long$')
  5992  (5307)                  RETURN
  5993  (5308)              ENDIF
  5994  (5309)  
  5995  (5310)              % LEAVE THE OLD ROOM FIRST. Without this the old room still counts
  5996  (5311)              % us as a member and still holds our seat: it never hears that we
  5997  (5312)              % went, and a room only gives a seat back on a clean leave or on a
  5998  (5313)              % reap. Two or three /joins and the room is full of people who are
  5999  (5314)              % not there. The reported "/join doesn't work" hid this one - the
  6000  (5315)              % join never reached a room, so the leak never had a chance to show.
  6001  (5316)              IF joined THEN
  6002  (5317)                  buildOwn(kLeave, 0) =: length
  6003  (5318)                  sendToServer(length)
  6004  (5319)              ENDIF
  6005  (5320)  
  6006  (5321)              % Re-point the room and send the join again. setRoom adds the
  6007  (5322)              % "CHAT-" prefix; the raw copy that stood here is the whole of the
  6008  (5323)              % /join defect.
  6009  (5324)              setRoom(at, argLen)
  6010  (5325)              FALSE =: joined
  6011  (5326)              0 =: serverMagic
  6012  (5327)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL8', 'joining ')
  6013  (5328)              showRoom
  6014  (5329)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL4', '...$')
  6015  (5330)              sendJoin
  6016  (5331)              RETURN
  6017  (5332)          ENDIF
  6018  (5333)  
  6019  (5334)          IF cmdIs('NICK') = 1 THEN
  6020  (5335)              6 =: at
  6021  (5336)              typedLen - at =: argLen
  6022  (5337)              IF argLen < 1 THEN
  6023  (5338)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL19', '/nick needs a name$')
  6024  (5339)                  RETURN
  6025  (5340)              ENDIF
  6026  (5341)              IF argLen > maxNameLen THEN
  6027  (5342)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL22', 'that name is too long$')
  6028  (5343)                  RETURN
  6029  (5344)              ENDIF
  6030  (5345)              % NOT JOINED? THEN THIS IS NOT A RENAME - IT IS A RETRY.
  6031  (5346)              %
  6032  (5347)              % This used to answer "not in the room yet" and stop, which
  6033  (5348)              % left a refused user with NO WAY OUT: the server turns a
  6034  (5349)              % duplicate nickname away, and the client then refused the one
  6035  (5350)              % command that could fix it. The only escape was to quit and
  6036  (5351)              % edit CHAT:CNFG by hand, which no end user will do.
  6037  (5352)              % MEASURED 2026-08-20 with two terminals on one SINTRAN user:
  6038  (5353)              % both read the same CHAT:CNFG, both wanted the same name, and
  6039  (5354)              % the second was stuck.
  6040  (5355)              %
  6041  (5356)              % Both halves were individually right - the server should
  6042  (5357)              % refuse a duplicate, and a rename normally IS a message to a
  6043  (5358)              % room. The combination was the trap.
  6044  (5359)              %
  6045  (5360)              % With no room to tell, changing the name is purely local:
  6046  (5361)              % take the new name and knock again.
  6047  (5362)              % FROM HERE ON, ANY REFUSAL IS ABOUT A NAME THE USER PICKED.
  6048  (5363)              %
  6049  (5364)              % Set BEFORE the name is even validated, because the intent is what
  6050  (5365)              % matters: once somebody has typed /nick, "that name is taken" means
  6051  (5366)              % somebody else has it - NOT that they are in the room twice. See the
  6052  (5367)              % kReject handler, which switches its whole message on this.
  6053  (5368)              TRUE =: userChoseNick
  6054  (5369)  
  6055  (5370)              % DRAIN FIRST, THEN DECIDE. A WELCOME MAY BE SITTING UNREAD.
  6056  (5371)              %
  6057  (5372)              % MEASURED 2026-08-22 with the server running: typing /nick straight
  6058  (5373)              % after starting the client printed
  6059  (5374)              %
  6060  (5375)              %     joining (LOBBY) as SYSTEM
  6061  (5376)              %     trying TESTER
  6062  (5377)              %     you are TESTER  (/nick to change)
  6063  (5378)              %     cannot join as TESTER - already joined
  6064  (5379)              %
  6065  (5380)              % "trying" is printed by the retry branch BELOW, so that branch ran -
  6066  (5381)              % which means `joined` was still FALSE. It was false only because the
  6067  (5382)              % first join's kWelcome had not been PROCESSED yet; `joined` is set
  6068  (5383)              % where kWelcome is handled. So the client sent a SECOND join and the
  6069  (5384)              % server refused it, correctly, as already joined.
  6070  (5385)              %
  6071  (5386)              % Nothing was wrong with either branch. The state they switch on was
  6072  (5387)              % simply out of date, because the welcome was still in the port. That
  6073  (5388)              % is easy to hit here: the client only renders new lines when RETURN
  6074  (5389)              % is pressed, so a welcome can be waiting while the user is typing.
  6075  (5390)              %
  6076  (5391)              % One drain pass costs nothing and settles it. It is deliberately
  6077  (5392)              % BEFORE the NOT joined test rather than inside it - the same staleness
  6078  (5393)              % would make a rename look like a retry OR a retry look like a rename.
  6079  (5394)              drainPort
  6080  (5395)  
  6081  (5396)              IF NOT joined THEN
  6082  (5397)                  setMyName(at, argLen)
  6083  (5398)  
  6084  (5399)                  % "TRYING", NOT "YOU ARE" - WE ARE NOT THAT NAME YET. This is a
  6085  (5400)                  % retry: the name goes into a fresh join and the SERVER decides.
  6086  (5401)                  % Announcing it here and then printing the server's refusal two
  6087  (5402)                  % lines later is what put
  6088  (5403)                  %     you are ANNA
  6089  (5404)                  %     OLAV: that nickname is taken
  6090  (5405)                  % on the screen - the client claiming a name, the server denying
  6091  (5406)                  % a different one, and nothing to connect the two.
  6092  (5407)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL7', 'trying ')
  6093  (5408)                  showMyName
  6094  (5409)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '$')
  6095  (5410)                  sendJoin
  6096  (5411)                  RETURN
  6097  (5412)              ENDIF
  6098  (5413)  
  6099  (5414)              % THE NEW NAME GOES IN THE NAME FIELD - see buildRename, which is
  6100  (5415)              % where the whole story of this being wrong the other way round is
  6101  (5416)              % written down. It reads the name straight out of typed, so there is
  6102  (5417)              % no shuffling to do here either.
  6103  (5418)              buildRename(at, argLen) =: length
  6104  (5419)              sendToServer(length)
  6105  (5420)  
  6106  (5421)              % Our own name changes only when the room says so, with kRenOk.
  6107  (5422)              RETURN
  6108  (5423)          ENDIF
  6109  (5424)  
  6110  (5425)          % /TELL NAME what to say - a message to ONE person.
  6111  (5426)          %
  6112  (5427)          % A SEAT IS REQUIRED, and not because the other person has to be in a
  6113  (5428)          % room - they do not, and that is half the point of the feature. It is
  6114  (5429)          % because the SENDER needs a name for the message to be from, which is
  6115  (5430)          % the same reason the server refuses this below its own seat test.
  6116  (5431)          % /w N - go to a window. Also /window.
  6117  (5432)          %
  6118  (5433)          % A COMMAND AS WELL AS A KEY, because the key cycles and cycling past
  6119  (5434)          % the one you want is annoying with four open. The command is also the
  6120  (5435)          % only form that works on a printing terminal, which has no bar to
  6121  (5436)          % look at but can still hold a conversation.
  6122  (5437)          IF cmdIs('W') = 1 OR cmdIs('WINDOW') = 1 THEN
  6123  (5438)              IF NOT screenOn THEN
  6124  (5439)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL', 'windows need a screen terminal$')
  6125  (5440)                  RETURN
  6126  (5441)              ENDIF
  6127  (5442)  
  6128  (5443)              % The digit is the last character typed, so a bare /w is a cycle
  6129  (5444)              % rather than an error - which is what a person who typed it
  6130  (5445)              % without thinking almost certainly meant.
  6131  (5446)              IF typedLen < 3 THEN
  6132  (5447)                  winNext
  6133  (5448)                  RETURN
  6134  (5449)              ENDIF
  6135  (5450)  
  6136  (5451)              typed(typedLen - 1) - 48 =: at
  6137  (5452)              IF at < 1 THEN
  6138  (5453)                  winNext
  6139  (5454)                  RETURN
  6140  (5455)              ENDIF
  6141  (5456)              IF at > maxWins THEN
  6142  (5457)                  winNext
  6143  (5458)                  RETURN
  6144  (5459)              ENDIF
  6145  (5460)              winGo(at)
  6146  (5461)              RETURN
  6147  (5462)          ENDIF
  6148  (5463)  
  6149  (5464)          % /close - put a conversation window away.
  6150  (5465)          %
  6151  (5466)          % THE ROOM CANNOT BE CLOSED. It is where a plain line goes when no
  6152  (5467)          % conversation is open, and a client with no window at all would have
  6153  (5468)          % nowhere to put an arriving room line.
  6154  (5469)          IF cmdIs('CLOSE') = 1 THEN
  6155  (5470)              IF curWin = 1 THEN
  6156  (5471)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL', 'the room window stays open$')
  6157  (5472)                  RETURN
  6158  (5473)              ENDIF
  6159  (5474)  
  6160  (5475)              0 =: winUsed(curWin)
  6161  (5476)              0 =: winCount(curWin)
  6162  (5477)              0 =: winLabLen(curWin)
  6163  (5478)              winGo(1)
  6164  (5479)  
  6165  (5480)              % THE BAR HAS LOST AN ENTRY even if winGo decided it had nothing
  6166  (5481)              % to do, so it is redrawn here - and only it. Nothing else on the
  6167  (5482)              % screen changed when a window went away.
  6168  (5483)              IF screenOn THEN
  6169  (5484)                  drawBar
  6170  (5485)                  drawInput
  6171  (5486)                  flush
  6172  (5487)              ENDIF
  6173  (5488)              RETURN
  6174  (5489)          ENDIF
  6175  (5490)  
  6176  (5491)          IF cmdIs('TELL') = 1 THEN
  6177  (5492)              IF NOT joined THEN
  6178  (5493)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL34', 'not in a room yet - wait or /join$')
  6179  (5494)                  RETURN
  6180  (5495)              ENDIF
  6181  (5496)  
  6182  (5497)              % 6 = past "/TELL ". Exactly "/TELL" is typedLen 5, so argLen comes
  6183  (5498)              % out negative - hence "< 1" rather than "= 0".
  6184  (5499)              6 =: at
  6185  (5500)              typedLen - at =: argLen
  6186  (5501)              IF argLen < 1 THEN
  6187  (5502)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL', '/tell NAME what to say - or D102!NAME$')
  6188  (5503)                  RETURN
  6189  (5504)              ENDIF
  6190  (5505)  
  6191  (5506)              % THE FIRST SPACE SEPARATES THE NAME FROM THE MESSAGE.
  6192  (5507)              0 - 1 =: cut
  6193  (5508)              FOR i IN 0:argLen - 1 DO
  6194  (5509)                  IF cut < 0 THEN
  6195  (5510)                      IF typed(at + i) = spaceByte(0) THEN
  6196  (5511)                          i =: cut
  6197  (5512)                      ENDIF
  6198  (5513)                  ENDIF
  6199  (5514)              ENDFOR
  6200  (5515)  
  6201  (5516)              IF cut < 1 THEN
  6202  (5517)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL', 'who to? /tell NAME what to say$')
  6203  (5518)                  RETURN
  6204  (5519)              ENDIF
  6205  (5520)              IF argLen - cut - 1 < 1 THEN
  6206  (5521)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL', 'nothing to say to them$')
  6207  (5522)                  RETURN
  6208  (5523)              ENDIF
  6209  (5524)  
  6210  (5525)              % A SLASH IN THE NAME WOULD SPLIT IN THE WRONG PLACE at the far
  6211  (5526)              % end, and the message would go to a name nobody has. Said out
  6212  (5527)              % loud rather than sent and lost.
  6213  (5528)              FOR i IN 0:cut - 1 DO
  6214  (5529)                  IF typed(at + i) = slashByte(0) THEN
  6215  (5530)                      OUTPUT(1, 'AL', 'a name cannot have a slash in it$')
  6216  (5531)                      RETURN
  6217  (5532)                  ENDIF
  6218  (5533)              ENDFOR
  6219  (5534)  
  6220  (5535)              % KEPT BEFORE IT IS SENT, so the confirmation has something to
  6221  (5536)              % show. See lastTell.
  6222  (5537)              argLen - cut - 1 =: lastTellLen
  6223  (5538)              IF lastTellLen > 76 THEN
  6224  (5539)                  76 =: lastTellLen
  6225  (5540)              ENDIF
  6226  (5541)              FOR i IN 1:lastTellLen DO
  6227  (5542)                  typed(at + cut + i) =: lastTell(i - 1)
  6228  (5543)              ENDFOR
  6229  (5544)  
  6230  (5545)              buildTell(at, cut, argLen - cut - 1) =: length
  6231  (5546)              sendToServer(length)
  6232  (5547)              RETURN
  6233  (5548)          ENDIF
  6234  (5549)  
  6235  (5550)          IF cmdIs('LEAVE') = 1 THEN
  6236  (5551)              IF NOT joined THEN
  6237  (5552)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL20', 'not in the room yet$')
  6238  (5553)                  RETURN
  6239  (5554)              ENDIF
  6240  (5555)              buildOwn(kLeave, 0) =: length
  6241  (5556)              sendToServer(length)
  6242  (5557)              FALSE =: joined
  6243  (5558)  
  6244  (5559)              % THE MAGIC IS KEPT ON PURPOSE, and clearing it here was a bug.
  6245  (5560)              %
  6246  (5561)              % serverMagic is the ADDRESS OF THE SERVER. Leaving a room does not
  6247  (5562)              % make the server unreachable - it only means we no longer hold a
  6248  (5563)              % seat in it. Zeroing it left the client with nothing to send to, so
  6249  (5564)              % every question that is supposed to work while unjoined silently
  6250  (5565)              % did nothing:
  6251  (5566)              %
  6252  (5567)              %   /list  - which the wire registry has always said is allowed
  6253  (5568)              %            unjoined, and which is exactly what somebody asks when
  6254  (5569)              %            they have just left and are deciding where to go;
  6255  (5570)              %   /who   - the all-rooms answer built on 2026-08-22.
  6256  (5571)              %
  6257  (5572)              % MEASURED: after /leave, /who printed nothing at all. The server
  6258  (5573)              % was answering correctly; the question never left the building.
  6259  (5574)              %
  6260  (5575)              % joined is what gates SPEAKING, and it still does - see the main
  6261  (5576)              % loop. That flag and the address are two different facts and were
  6262  (5577)              % being cleared as if they were one.
  6263  (5578)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL14', 'left the room$')
  6264  (5579)              RETURN
  6265  (5580)          ENDIF
  6266  (5581)  
  6267  (5582)          % /exit IS THE NAME, /quit STILL WORKS.
  6268  (5583)          %
  6269  (5584)          % The help lists only /exit, so there is one answer to "how do I get
  6270  (5585)          % out" - but /quit has been in this client since it was written and in
  6271  (5586)          % the screen prototype beside it, and silently removing a word people
  6272  (5587)          % have in their fingers is a poor trade for tidiness. It costs one
  6273  (5588)          % test.
  6274  (5589)          %
  6275  (5590)          % Neither is a prefix of any other command, so /e and /q both work.
  6276  (5591)          IF cmdIs('EXIT') = 1 OR cmdIs('QUIT') = 1 THEN
  6277  (5592)              IF joined THEN
  6278  (5593)                  buildOwn(kLeave, 0) =: length
  6279  (5594)                  sendToServer(length)
  6280  (5595)              ENDIF
  6281  (5596)              FALSE =: running
  6282  (5597)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL4', 'bye$')
  6283  (5598)              RETURN
  6284  (5599)          ENDIF
  6285  (5600)  
  6286  (5601)          OUTPUT(1, 'AL28', 'no such command - try /help$')
  6287  (5602)      ENDROUTINE
  6288  (5603)  
  6289  (5604)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6290  (5605)      % The program.
  6291  (5606)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6292  (5607)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6293  (5608)      % The prompt: WHO you are and WHERE you are, on the line you type into.
  6294  (5609)      %
  6295  (5610)      %     [OLAV]           before any room has been entered
  6296  (5611)      %     [OLAV/LOBBY]     once the server has welcomed us into one
  6297  (5612)      %
  6298  (5613)      % A prompt is not decoration here. Without one the program gives no sign it
  6299  (5614)      % is waiting, and with arriving lines landing on the same screen there was
  6300  (5615)      % nothing to tell a typed line from a received one.
  6301  (5616)      %
  6302  (5617)      % NOT YET: [OLAV/Private:ANNA] for a private channel with one other user.
  6303  (5618)      % There is no private channel in this client to be in - no /msg, no second
  6304  (5619)      % conversation - so a prompt claiming one would be inventing a state that
  6305  (5620)      % does not exist. It goes in when the thing it names does.
  6306  (5621)      %
  6307  (5622)      % showRoom is NOT used here: it wraps the name in parentheses for prose
  6308  (5623)      % like "joining (LOBBY)", and "[OLAV/(LOBBY)]" is one bracket too many.
  6309  (5624)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6310  (5625)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : showPrompt
  6311  (5626)          INTEGER : i
  6312  (5627)  
  6313  (5628)          OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '[')
  6314  (5629)          showMyName
  6315  (5630)          IF joined THEN
  6316  (5631)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '/')
  6317  (5632)              FOR i IN 1:lenRoomName DO
  6318  (5633)                  roomName(i - 1) =: oneChar(0)
  6319  (5634)                  OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', oneChar)
  6320  (5635)              ENDFOR
  6321  (5636)          ENDIF
  6322  (5637)          OUTPUT(1, 'AL2', '] ')
  6323  (5638)  
  6324  (5639)          % PUT BACK WHAT WAS ALREADY TYPED.
  6325  (5640)          %
  6326  (5641)          % An arriving line is printed on the line being typed into - there is
  6327  (5642)          % one cursor and the room does not wait for anyone to finish a
  6328  (5643)          % sentence. Without this, a message arriving mid-sentence silently eats
  6329  (5644)          % half of it: the characters are still in the buffer and will still be
  6330  (5645)          % sent, but the screen shows an empty prompt, so the user retypes what
  6331  (5646)          % they had and sends it twice.
  6332  (5647)          FOR i IN 1:typedLen DO
  6333  (5648)              typed(i - 1) =: oneChar(0)
  6334  (5649)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', oneChar)
  6335  (5650)          ENDFOR
  6336  (5651)      ENDROUTINE
  6337  (5652)  
  6338  (5653)      PROGRAM : chatClient
  6339  (5654)          XMMSGIDENTIFIER : msgIdent
  6340  (5655)          INTEGER : length
  6341  (5656)          INTEGER : i                   % the start-up room copy below uses it
  6342  (5657)          % How many bytes were still waiting when the keyboard was taken.
  6343  (5658)          % Screen mode only - see the drain beside the MON3 that turns the
  6344  (5659)          % echo off.
  6345  (5660)          INTEGER : drained
  6346  (5661)  
  6347  (5662)          INISTACK stack
  6348  (5663)  
  6349  (5664)          % WINDOW 1 IS THE ROOM AND IT ALWAYS EXISTS. Everything else in the
  6350  (5665)          % table stays zero until a conversation opens one, and zero is what
  6351  (5666)          % winUsed reads as "not there" - so no other seeding is needed.
  6352  (5667)          1 =: winUsed(1)
  6353  (5668)          1 =: curWin
  6354  (5669)  
  6355  (5670)          xmpfopn(0, myPort) =: returnStatus
  6356  (5671)          IF returnStatus >< XMOK THEN
  6357  (5672)              showStatus('xmpfopn', returnStatus)
  6358  (5673)              RETURN
  6359  (5674)          ENDIF
  6360  (5675)  
  6361  (5676)          % A "port = N magic= M st= S" line used to print here, from a XMPFP2M
  6362  (5677)          % call kept only to feed it. It was there to answer one question: the
  6363  (5678)          % server was receiving the join, parsing it exactly right and answering
  6364  (5679)          % with status 0, and nothing was arriving back - so was the reply going
  6365  (5680)          % to the wrong address?
  6366  (5681)          %
  6367  (5682)          % IT WAS NOT. The reply address was never the bug. The two magic numbers
  6368  (5683)          % matched, which is what sent the search to the receive test in drainPort
  6369  (5684)          % where the real fault was: XMPFRCV returned XMOK, the test excluded
  6370  (5685)          % zero, and the welcome was dequeued and dropped. That is the whole
  6371  (5686)          % reason this diagnostic earned its keep, and the reason it can now go.
  6372  (5687)          %
  6373  (5688)          % XMPFP2M went with it. Nothing else read myMagic.
  6374  (5689)  
  6375  (5690)          % ===================================================================
  6376  (5691)          % HOW THIS CLIENT WATCHES TWO THINGS WITHOUT A NO-WAIT TERMINAL.
  6377  (5692)          %
  6378  (5693)          % The obvious design polls both: switch the terminal to no-wait with
  6379  (5694)          % TNOWAIT (307B) so INBT stops blocking, then spin on keyboard and port
  6380  (5695)          % together.
  6381  (5696)          %
  6382  (5697)          % ============================================================ SOLVED ==
  6383  (5698)          % EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS KEPT AS A RECORD OF A WRONG CONCLUSION.
  6384  (5699)          % It argued at length that a no-wait terminal could not be had here, and
  6385  (5700)          % that the client should therefore let the keyboard block. That decision
  6386  (5701)          % is what made an arriving line invisible until the user pressed return,
  6387  (5702)          % which on 2026-08-20 looked exactly like a broadcast reaching nobody
  6388  (5703)          % and cost an afternoon.
  6389  (5704)          %
  6390  (5705)          % THE WAY THROUGH IS MONITOR_CALL BY NAME, which the reasoning below
  6391  (5706)          % dismisses because "the MON-CALL-NAMES file was not found". That file
  6392  (5707)          % is supplied by MON-CALL-1B-A00, which is now INSTALLED and is already
  6393  (5708)          % in this client's LOAD list - CHATSV has been calling
  6394  (5709)          % MONITOR_CALL('TimeOut', ...) with it for weeks. And the call wanted is
  6395  (5710)          % not TNOWAIT (307B) at all but NoWaitSwitch (NOWT 36B), whose three
  6396  (5711)          % parameters are ALL INPUTS, so there is not even an output to get
  6397  (5712)          % wrong. See terminalNoWait and terminalWait above.
  6398  (5713)          %
  6399  (5714)          % The lesson is the one that keeps recurring on this project: a "cannot"
  6400  (5715)          % written down once outlives the thing that made it true. This note
  6401  (5716)          % survived the installation of the very package that answers it.
  6402  (5717)          % ======================================================================
  6403  (5718)          %
  6404  (5719)          % The old reasoning, for the record:
  6405  (5720)          %
  6406  (5721)          %   THE PORT IS DRAINED WITHOUT WAITING - xmpfrcv with flags ZERO.
  6407  (5722)          %   That is XMSG'S OWN no-wait and has nothing to do with the terminal:
  6408  (5723)          %   without XFWTF the call returns at once whether or not a message was
  6409  (5724)          %   there. So everything waiting can be taken and printed in a tight
  6410  (5725)          %   loop that never blocks.
  6411  (5726)          %
  6412  (5727)          %   THE KEYBOARD IS ALLOWED TO BLOCK - MON1, one character at a time,
  6413  (5728)          %   until carriage return.
  6414  (5729)          %
  6415  (5730)          % The one thing it costs: arriving lines are shown when you finish
  6416  (5731)          % yours, not the instant they land. Press RETURN on an empty line to
  6417  (5732)          % look. That is the whole price, and it buys a client that needs no
  6418  (5733)          % missing product, no assembler and no interface routine.
  6419  (5734)          %
  6420  (5735)          % Reading the keyboard needs INBT (1B). Getting a NO-WAIT terminal
  6421  (5736)          % would additionally need TNOWAIT (307B), and that is the one that
  6422  (5737)          % cannot be had:
  6423  (5738)          %
  6424  (5739)          % MONITOR_CALL IS THE WRONG TOOL, both ways round, and both were tried:
  6425  (5740)          %
  6426  (5741)          %   By NAME - Monitor_Call('InByte', ...), the form the Monitor Calls
  6427  (5742)          %   manual prints for PLANC - needs the MON-CALL-NAMES file. The
  6428  (5743)          %   compiler answers THE MON-CALL-NAMES FILE WAS NOT FOUND, and no such
  6429  (5744)          %   file exists under any of the nine users on this machine.
  6430  (5745)          %
  6431  (5746)          %   By NUMBER - MONITOR_CALL(1B, ...) - compiles and then does not
  6432  (5747)          %   link: it generates a call to 5MON_P, which is in neither
  6433  (5748)          %   PLANC-1BANK-F00 nor PLANC-2BANK-F00 (LIST-ENTRIES-UNDEFINED after
  6434  (5749)          %   loading both).
  6435  (5750)          %
  6436  (5751)          % WHAT THE PLANC MANUAL ACTUALLY SAYS (ND-60.117.5, appendix 0.2,
  6437  (5752)          % "Monitor Calls Available on the ND-100 and the ND-500"): the runtime
  6438  (5753)          % provides monitor calls as ORDINARY ROUTINES, one per call, named MONn
  6439  (5754)          % and IMPORTed like anything else -
  6440  (5755)          %
  6441  (5756)          %     ROUTINE VOID, BYTE (INTEGER) : MON1 (dev)      % INBIT
  6442  (5757)          %
  6443  (5758)          % which is why the COSMOS guide's own sample opens with
  6444  (5759)          % IMPORT (ROUTINE VOID, VOID: MONO) - that is MON0, LEAVE. These are
  6445  (5760)          % the MN1, MN122, MN310 ... units sitting in PLANC-1BANK-F00. So INBT
  6446  (5761)          % is reachable, correctly, as MON1(1) - no MONITOR_CALL, no names file,
  6447  (5762)          % no 5MON_P.
  6448  (5763)          %
  6449  (5764)          % TNOWAIT IS NOT. The appendix lists MON 0,1,2,3,4,11,12,17,21,45,47,
  6450  (5765)          % 50,54,62,63,64,65,104,113,114,117,120,144,161,162,167,263 - and 307
  6451  (5766)          % is not among them. The manual says what to do about that in as many
  6452  (5767)          % words: "Any monitor calls not listed here, may be called from a PLANC
  6453  (5768)          % routine if a suitable interface routine is constructed by the user.
  6454  (5769)          % If this is done, the user must load the interface routine BEFORE the
  6455  (5770)          % PLANC runtime library."
  6456  (5771)          %
  6457  (5772)          % A hand-written interface routine for MON 307B, loaded ahead of
  6458  (5773)          % PLANC-1BANK-F00, is what the manual says to do about that.
  6459  (5774)          %
  6460  (5775)          % THIS COMMENT USED TO SAY THAT WAS IMPOSSIBLE HERE. It claimed "D100 has
  6461  (5776)          % neither MAC nor NPL installed (LIST-FILES found only MACM-AREA:DATA)".
  6462  (5777)          % BOTH HALVES ARE WRONG, and the way they were wrong is worth keeping.
  6463  (5778)          %
  6464  (5779)          % LIST-FILES IS THE WRONG COMMAND FOR THIS QUESTION. Compilers and other
  6465  (5780)          % subsystems are loaded into shared memory across users, not kept as :PROG
  6466  (5781)          % files in anybody's area, so LIST-FILES was never going to find one and
  6467  (5782)          % its silence proved nothing. The command that answers it is
  6468  (5783)          % LIST-REENTRANT. On D100, 2026-08-18:
  6469  (5784)          %
  6470  (5785)          %   START RESTART SEGMENT   NAME
  6471  (5786)          %  177777B 177776B    107B   MAC
  6472  (5787)          %      0B      1B    110B   NPL
  6473  (5788)          %    752B    752B    111B   PLANC-100
  6474  (5789)          %     0B      0B    112B   PED
  6475  (5790)          %    11B     11B    113B   FORTRAN-100
  6476  (5791)          %
  6477  (5792)          % MAC and NPL are BOTH there, alongside the PLANC that builds this program.
  6478  (5793)          % @MAC answers "- MAC -".
  6479  (5794)          %
  6480  (5795)          % So the door the manual describes is open: any monitor call outside the
  6481  (5796)          % runtime's ready-made MONn set can be reached with an interface routine
  6482  (5797)          % assembled in MAC and loaded BEFORE the PLANC library. TNOWAIT (307B) is
  6483  (5798)          % the one this program wanted, and with it the loop below could stop being
  6484  (5799)          % a busy one - see "what is not finished" at the foot of the file.
  6485  (5800)          %
  6486  (5801)          % The design below still stands on its own: it needs no interface routine,
  6487  (5802)          % and it works. But it is now a CHOICE rather than the only option, and
  6488  (5803)          % nobody should read this comment and conclude the machine cannot do it.
  6489  (5804)          % ===================================================================
  6490  (5805)  
  6491  (5806)          % SETTLE THE NAME BEFORE THE JOIN, because the join CARRIES it and
  6492  (5807)          % there is no second chance to put one in. Nothing is asked of the
  6493  (5808)          % user - see setupName.
  6494  (5809)          setupName
  6495  (5810)  
  6496  (5811)          % THE DEFAULT ROOM, BUILT BY THE SAME RULE AS A TYPED /join.
  6497  (5812)          %
  6498  (5813)          % This client joins a room at start-up whether or not the user asked
  6499  (5814)          % for one, and the bug report was right that nothing said so. It is
  6500  (5815)          % said now, with the name in parentheses.
  6501  (5816)          %
  6502  (5817)          % The short name goes through "typed" because setRoom reads from there.
  6503  (5818)          % That is deliberate: the prefix is then added in ONE place for both
  6504  (5819)          % paths, and the start-up name cannot drift from the /join name.
  6505  (5820)          FOR i IN 1:lenHomeRoom DO
  6506  (5821)              homeRoom(i - 1) =: typed(i - 1)
  6507  (5822)          ENDFOR
  6508  (5823)          setRoom(0, lenHomeRoom)
  6509  (5824)  
  6510  (5825)          % ---- WHICH RENDERER? Asked ONCE, here, and never again -----------
  6511  (5826)          %
  6512  (5827)          % blankscreen is VTM's first call and CTYTP is only valid AFTER it -
  6513  (5828)          % ask before and the bits are whatever was in memory. Two bits decide
  6514  (5829)          % it: the terminal is a VDU at all, and it can be told where to put
  6515  (5830)          % the cursor. Without both, a screen renderer would draw its frame as
  6516  (5831)          % a page of line noise on a printing terminal.
  6517  (5832)          %
  6518  (5833)          % ASKED ONCE because a renderer that could change under the program
  6519  (5834)          % would need every drawing site to cope with both, which is how a
  6520  (5835)          % screen ends up half painted.
  6521  (5836)          blankscreen
  6522  (5837)          MON16(1) =: termType
  6523  (5838)          TRUE =: screenOn
  6524  (5839)          IF (termType AND bitVdu) = 0 THEN
  6525  (5840)              FALSE =: screenOn
  6526  (5841)          ENDIF
  6527  (5842)          IF (termType AND bitCpos) = 0 THEN
  6528  (5843)              FALSE =: screenOn
  6529  (5844)          ENDIF
  6530  (5845)  
  6531  (5846)          IF screenOn THEN
  6532  (5847)              % TAKE THE KEYBOARD BEFORE A SINGLE THING IS DRAWN.
  6533  (5848)              %
  6534  (5849)              % No echo, because a raw key loop has no say in where the cursor
  6535  (5850)              % is and SINTRAN would drop each character wherever the last
  6536  (5851)              % drawing finished - inside the windows. MEASURED in CHATUI: with
  6537  (5852)              % this done after the first paint, every run came up showing
  6538  (5853)              % '> ??g??', and draining could not fix it because draining empties
  6539  (5854)              % the BUFFER and not the SCREEN it was already echoed to.
  6540  (5855)              %
  6541  (5856)              % And no ESC abort, which is what makes turning the echo off SAFE:
  6542  (5857)              % an abort would skip the exit path and hand back a terminal with
  6543  (5858)              % no echo at all.
  6544  (5859)              MON3(1, -1, echoTab)
  6545  (5860)              MON71(1)
  6546  (5861)  
  6547  (5862)              % WHATEVER IS ALREADY WAITING IS NOT THE USER TALKING TO THIS
  6548  (5863)              % PROGRAM - it is the tail of whatever was typed to get here, and
  6549  (5864)              % on a line whose terminal type was unset, the answer to VTM's own
  6550  (5865)              % question about it.
  6551  (5866)              DO
  6552  (5867)                  MON66(1) =: drained
  6553  (5868)                  WHILE drained > 0
  6554  (5869)                  MON1(1) =: drained
  6555  (5870)              ENDDO
  6556  (5871)          ENDIF
  6557  (5872)  
  6558  (5873)          % WHAT IS BEING ATTEMPTED, not what has been achieved. The name here is
  6559  (5874)          % the one the join CARRIES; whether we get to keep it is the server's
  6560  (5875)          % answer, printed when the welcome arrives.
  6561  (5876)          OUTPUT(1, 'AL8', 'joining ')
  6562  (5877)          showRoom
  6563  (5878)          OUTPUT(1, 'AL4', ' as ')
  6564  (5879)          showMyName
  6565  (5880)          OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '$')
  6566  (5881)          sendJoin
  6567  (5882)  
  6568  (5883)          % ONE LINE, NOT THE WHOLE COMMAND LIST. Printing all ten commands at
  6569  (5884)          % start-up buries the two lines that actually matter under a screen
  6570  (5885)          % nobody asked for.
  6571  (5886)          OUTPUT(1, 'AL37', 'Type /HELP for help and command list$')
  6572  (5887)  
  6573  (5888)          % showPrompt re-echoes whatever is in the typed buffer, so the buffer
  6574  (5889)          % has to MEAN something before the first prompt. Nothing above sets it,
  6575  (5890)          % and PLANC does not promise a module variable starts at zero.
  6576  (5891)          0 =: typedLen
  6577  (5892)          TRUE =: inputDirty
  6578  (5893)  
  6579  (5894)          % THE FIRST PAINT GOES OVER THE JOIN BANNER, which is the point: those
  6580  (5895)          % lines were printed before the renderer was known and they are the
  6581  (5896)          % LINE renderer's, so on a screen they are simply painted over.
  6582  (5897)          IF screenOn THEN
  6583  (5898)              drawAll
  6584  (5899)              flush
  6585  (5900)          ELSE
  6586  (5901)              showPrompt
  6587  (5902)          ENDIF
  6588  (5903)  
  6589  (5904)          % FROM HERE THE TERMINAL IS IN NO-WAIT, so MON1 stops blocking and the
  6590  (5905)          % loop below can look at the port and the keyboard in turn. Switched on
  6591  (5906)          % AFTER the join is sent, so nothing above has to care, and switched off
  6592  (5907)          % again on the way out - see the end of the loop.
  6593  (5908)          %
  6594  (5909)          % ASK WHICH TERMINAL THIS IS FIRST. terminalNoWait needs the LDN and
  6595  (5910)          % there is no way to know it without asking - the number differs per
  6596  (5911)          % session and per machine, and the value the program used to pass was
  6597  (5912)          % not a terminal number at all.
  6598  (5913)          findMyLdn
  6599  (5914)          terminalNoWait
  6600  (5915)  
  6601  (5916)          % ONE LAST DRAIN, AND THIS ONE IS THE ONE THAT WORKS.
  6602  (5917)          %
  6603  (5918)          % There is already a drain up beside the MON3 that turns the echo off,
  6604  (5919)          % and it is not enough: the input line still came up holding '??g??'.
  6605  (5920)          % Those bytes arrive DURING the drawing, not before it - VTM asks the
  6606  (5921)          % line what terminal it is and the answer lands in the buffer while the
  6607  (5922)          % frame is being painted, so a drain that ran earlier could not have
  6608  (5923)          % caught them.
  6609  (5924)          %
  6610  (5925)          % Draining here, with the screen already up and immediately before the
  6611  (5926)          % first read, is the last moment anything can arrive unasked. typedLen
  6612  (5927)          % is cleared after it because a byte that slipped past the first drain
  6613  (5928)          % is already sitting in the buffer, and clearing without draining would
  6614  (5929)          % just let it back in on the next pass.
  6615  (5930)          IF screenOn THEN
  6616  (5931)              DO
  6617  (5932)                  MON66(1) =: drained
  6618  (5933)                  WHILE drained > 0
  6619  (5934)                  MON1(1) =: drained
  6620  (5935)              ENDDO
  6621  (5936)              0 =: typedLen
  6622  (5937)              TRUE =: inputDirty
  6623  (5938)              drawInput
  6624  (5939)              flush
  6625  (5940)          ENDIF
  6626  (5941)  
  6627  (5942)          DO
  6628  (5943)              % ---- take everything the port is holding, without waiting -------
  6629  (5944)              drainPort
  6630  (5945)  
  6631  (5946)              % ---- then take whatever HAS been typed, without waiting ---------
  6632  (5947)              % MON1 CAN RAISE A ROUTINEERROR, AND IT MUST BE CAUGHT HERE.
  6633  (5948)              %
  6634  (5949)              % Measured on D100: without this handler the client printed the
  6635  (5950)              % same arriving message for ever and ESC broke it out of a SPIN,
  6636  (5951)              % not out of a wait - so MON1 was not blocking at all. The terminal
  6637  (5952)              % is in no-wait BECAUSE terminalNoWait ABOVE PUT IT THERE, and that
  6638  (5953)              % call is new: for a long time this comment asserted the no-wait
  6639  (5954)              % state while nothing in the program ever asked for it, so MON1
  6640  (5955)              % really did block and the loop only came round when the user
  6641  (5956)              % pressed return. The Monitor Calls manual says what no-wait means:
  6642  (5957)              % "In No Wait, the program does not wait for input or output to
  6643  (5958)              % complete. Monitor calls like InByte return ERROR CODE 3 instead."
  6644  (5959)              % In PLANC that arrives as a ROUTINEERROR, and an unhandled one
  6645  (5960)              % unwinds the program - which re-entered, re-sent the join, and
  6646  (5961)              % produced another undeliverable letter coming back. One missing
  6647  (5962)              % handler, three symptoms.
  6648  (5963)              %
  6649  (5964)              % Caught, it is exactly the poll the design wanted: ErrCode 3 means
  6650  (5965)              % nothing was typed, so leave ch at zero and let the loop go round
  6651  (5966)              % to look at the port again. ErrCode is supplied by the runtime and
  6652  (5967)              % must NOT be declared.
  6653  (5968)              % lineReady stays FALSE until RETURN is seen. typedLen is NOT reset
  6654  (5969)              % here: a line half typed when the port needs draining has to
  6655  (5970)              % survive the trip round the outer loop, so it is only cleared once
  6656  (5971)              % the line has been dealt with.
  6657  (5972)              FALSE =: lineReady
  6658  (5973)              DO
  6659  (5974)                  0 =: ch
  6660  (5975)                  ON ROUTINEERROR DO
  6661  (5976)                      IF ErrCode >< emptyInput THEN
  6662  (5977)                          OUTPUT(1, 'AL21', 'terminal read failed$')
  6663  (5978)                      ENDIF
  6664  (5979)                      0 =: rawCh
  6665  (5980)                  ENDON
  6666  (5981)                  % READ THE SAME DEVICE THE SWITCH WAS SET ON.
  6667  (5982)                  %
  6668  (5983)                  % MEASURED ON D100 2026-08-22, and this is the second half of
  6669  (5984)                  % the fix. Passing the real LDN to TerminalNoWait changed its
  6670  (5985)                  % status from -1 to 0 - a genuine change, the call is now
  6671  (5986)                  % accepted - and MON1 WENT ON BLOCKING ANYWAY. Everything was
  6672  (5987)                  % still queued until a keystroke flushed it.
  6673  (5988)                  %
  6674  (5989)                  % Because the switch was set on the terminal's LDN while the
  6675  (5990)                  % read was still asking for FILE 1. SINTRAN's "own terminal"
  6676  (5991)                  % shortcut and the logical device are not the same object to
  6677  (5992)                  % point a no-wait flag at, so the flag was on something this
  6678  (5993)                  % read never touched.
  6679  (5994)                  %
  6680  (5995)                  % myLdn falls back to 1, so a machine where ExecutionInfo does
  6681  (5996)                  % not answer reads exactly as before.
  6682  (5997)                  %
  6683  (5998)                  % SCREEN MODE ASKS MON66 FIRST AND ONLY THEN READS, and that is
  6684  (5999)                  % not a tidier way of doing the same thing - it is the fix for a
  6685  (6000)                  % client that joined and then went deaf.
  6686  (6001)                  %
  6687  (6002)                  % MEASURED on D100 2026-08-26. With the echo turned off, MON1
  6688  (6003)                  % BLOCKS whatever TerminalNoWait was told. The main loop sat in
  6689  (6004)                  % the read, drainPort never ran, and the server's messages came
  6690  (6005)                  % back undeliverable: SEATS said 1/16 - the client was properly
  6691  (6006)                  % seated - while bounce climbed on every line sent to it. The
  6692  (6007)                  % screen only ever changed when a key was pressed, because a
  6693  (6008)                  % keystroke was the only thing that let the loop go round.
  6694  (6009)                  %
  6695  (6010)                  % Nothing on screen says "deaf". The seat count and the bounce
  6696  (6011)                  % counter do, which is why they were the thing to look at.
  6697  (6012)                  %
  6698  (6013)                  % MON66 is ISIZE: how many bytes are WAITING. Ask that and MON1
  6699  (6014)                  % is only ever called when there is certainly one to take, so it
  6700  (6015)                  % cannot block. This is the same poll TESTUI and CHATUI use, and
  6701  (6016)                  % the reason the no-wait route was abandoned there too.
  6702  (6017)                  0 =: rawCh
  6703  (6018)                  IF screenOn THEN
  6704  (6019)                      MON66(1) =: drained
  6705  (6020)                      IF drained > 0 THEN
  6706  (6021)                          MON1(myLdn) =: rawCh
  6707  (6022)                      ENDIF
  6708  (6023)                  ELSE
  6709  (6024)                      MON1(myLdn) =: rawCh
  6710  (6025)                  ENDIF
  6711  (6026)                  % PARITY. Terminal input carries an even-parity bit in bit 7,
  6712  (6027)                  % so carriage return arrives as 141, NOT 13. Compare without
  6713  (6028)                  % masking and the client never sees the user press return and
  6714  (6029)                  % looks hung rather than wrong.
  6715  (6030)                  rawCh =: ch
  6716  (6031)                  ch AND parityMask =: ch
  6717  (6032)  
  6718  (6033)                  % ANY KEY CLOSES THE PANEL, AND DOES NOTHING ELSE.
  6719  (6034)                  %
  6720  (6035)                  % Consumed here, before every other test, so the key that
  6721  (6036)                  % dismisses the window cannot also be typed into the input
  6722  (6037)                  % line or read as a command. Anything that arrived while the
  6723  (6038)                  % panel was up is in the cache already and appears the moment
  6724  (6039)                  % the room is painted back.
  6725  (6040)                  IF panelUp THEN
  6726  (6041)                      IF ch >< 0 THEN
  6727  (6042)                          % closePanel, NOT drawAll. It clears the rectangle,
  6728  (6043)                          % puts back the separators, the pane rows and the one
  6729  (6044)                          % or two single rows that were actually underneath, and
  6730  (6045)                          % flushes. drawAll here rebuilt all 24 rows to undo a
  6731  (6046)                          % window covering a fraction of them.
  6732  (6047)                          closePanel
  6733  (6048)                          0 =: ch
  6734  (6049)                      ENDIF
  6735  (6050)                  ENDIF
  6736  (6051)  
  6737  (6052)                  % ONE KEY CYCLES THE WINDOWS.
  6738  (6053)                  %
  6739  (6054)                  % Control-W, because the letter matches the /w command and a
  6740  (6055)                  % control character cannot be part of anything somebody is
  6741  (6056)                  % typing - a plain letter would have to be a mode, and a mode
  6742  (6057)                  % is the thing that makes a terminal program feel haunted.
  6743  (6058)                  %
  6744  (6059)                  % 23 is Control-W after the parity mask above.
  6745  (6060)                  IF ch = 23 THEN
  6746  (6061)                      IF screenOn THEN
  6747  (6062)                          winNext
  6748  (6063)                      ENDIF
  6749  (6064)                      0 =: ch
  6750  (6065)                  ENDIF
  6751  (6066)  
  6752  (6067)                  IF ch = carriageRet THEN
  6753  (6068)                      TRUE =: lineReady
  6754  (6069)  
  6755  (6070)                      % A CARRIAGE RETURN IS NOT A NEW LINE ON THIS TERMINAL. It
  6756  (6071)                      % returns the cursor to column one of the line just typed
  6757  (6072)                      % and nothing moves it down, so whatever is printed next
  6758  (6073)                      % lands ON TOP of what the user wrote. That is what put
  6759  (6074)                      %     /whoIT-PROOF-LINE
  6760  (6075)                      % on the screen - "/who" with the next arriving line
  6761  (6076)                      % written over it - and it reads as the program mangling
  6762  (6077)                      % the input when in fact it simply never ended the line.
  6763  (6078)                      % One end-of-line here, the instant return is pressed.
  6764  (6079)                      %
  6765  (6080)                      % LINE MODE ONLY. On a screen there is no cursor wandering
  6766  (6081)                      % down the page - the input line is a fixed row that gets
  6767  (6082)                      % repainted, and an end-of-line written into it would scroll
  6768  (6083)                      % the whole frame up by one.
  6769  (6084)                      IF NOT screenOn THEN
  6770  (6085)                          OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '$')
  6771  (6086)                      ENDIF
  6772  (6087)                  ENDIF
  6773  (6088)  
  6774  (6089)                  % Leave the moment the keyboard runs dry (ch = 0, nothing was
  6775  (6090)                  % typed) or the line is finished. Going round again on an empty
  6776  (6091)                  % buffer is what turns this into a spin instead of a poll.
  6777  (6092)                  WHILE ch >< 0 AND NOT lineReady
  6778  (6093)  
  6779  (6094)                  % BACKSPACE, and it only has to be handled in SCREEN mode.
  6780  (6095)                  %
  6781  (6096)                  % On a printing terminal SINTRAN's own echo does the erasing
  6782  (6097)                  % before this program ever sees the line, which is why the test
  6783  (6098)                  % below has always been "> 31" and nobody missed it. With the
  6784  (6099)                  % echo turned off there is nobody else to do it.
  6785  (6100)                  %
  6786  (6101)                  % 127 as well as 8: VTM hands back 127 for the backspace key -
  6787  (6102)                  % MEASURED with KEYPROB, where a sent 0x08 came back as 127.
  6788  (6103)                  IF screenOn THEN
  6789  (6104)                      IF ch = 8 OR ch = 127 THEN
  6790  (6105)                          IF typedLen > 0 THEN
  6791  (6106)                              typedLen - 1 =: typedLen
  6792  (6107)                              TRUE =: inputDirty
  6793  (6108)                          ENDIF
  6794  (6109)                      ENDIF
  6795  (6110)                  ENDIF
  6796  (6111)  
  6797  (6112)                  IF ch > 31 AND ch < 127 THEN
  6798  (6113)                      % The bound. A full line stops accepting characters rather
  6799  (6114)                      % than writing past the end of typed - PLANC checks no array
  6800  (6115)                      % bound, so this test is the only thing between a held-down
  6801  (6116)                      % key and the variables laid out after it.
  6802  (6117)                      IF typedLen < maxTyped THEN
  6803  (6118)                          ch =: typed(typedLen)
  6804  (6119)                          typedLen + 1 =: typedLen
  6805  (6120)                          TRUE =: inputDirty
  6806  (6121)                      ENDIF
  6807  (6122)                  ENDIF
  6808  (6123)  
  6809  (6124)                  % SHOW WHAT HAS BEEN TYPED - but only once the burst is over.
  6810  (6125)                  %
  6811  (6126)                  % Repainting per character is not just slow, it is WRONG on a
  6812  (6127)                  % paste: the repaint costs more than the terminal takes to
  6813  (6128)                  % deliver the next byte, SINTRAN's 64-byte input buffer fills,
  6814  (6129)                  % and the tail is dropped - and the tail is where the RETURN
  6815  (6130)                  % is. Asking MON66 whether more is waiting is what tells us the
  6816  (6131)                  % burst has not finished. MEASURED in CHATUI: 63 characters and
  6817  (6132)                  % a RETURN arrived whole, 72 lost the RETURN.
  6818  (6133)                  IF screenOn THEN
  6819  (6134)                      IF NOT lineReady THEN
  6820  (6135)                          MON66(1) =: drained
  6821  (6136)                          IF drained <= 0 THEN
  6822  (6137)                              drawInput
  6823  (6138)                              flush
  6824  (6139)                          ENDIF
  6825  (6140)                      ENDIF
  6826  (6141)                  ENDIF
  6827  (6142)              ENDDO
  6828  (6143)  
  6829  (6144)              IF lineReady AND typedLen > 0 THEN
  6830  (6145)                  % NOTHING IS SENT BEFORE THE WELCOME. serverMagic is not known
  6831  (6146)                  % until then, so sending here would post the line to address
  6832  (6147)                  % zero. ChatClient.Say refuses the same way; this is that test.
  6833  (6148)                  % A LEADING SLASH MAKES IT A COMMAND, not something to say.
  6834  (6149)                  IF typed(0) = 47 THEN
  6835  (6150)                      handleCommand
  6836  (6151)                  ELSE
  6837  (6152)                      IF joined THEN
  6838  (6153)  
  6839  (6154)                          % WHERE A PLAIN LINE GOES DEPENDS ON THE WINDOW, and
  6840  (6155)                          % this is the single most important rule in the whole
  6841  (6156)                          % window feature.
  6842  (6157)                          %
  6843  (6158)                          % In a conversation window it goes to THAT PERSON. If
  6844  (6159)                          % it went to the room instead, somebody reading a
  6845  (6160)                          % private exchange and replying to it would broadcast
  6846  (6161)                          % their reply to everybody - a privacy failure caused
  6847  (6162)                          % entirely by the display, which is the exact thing the
  6848  (6163)                          % stars on a direct message exist to prevent. Getting
  6849  (6164)                          % this wrong would be worse than not having windows.
  6850  (6165)                          IF curWin = 1 THEN
  6851  (6166)                              buildOwn(kSay, typedLen) =: length
  6852  (6167)                              sendToServer(length)
  6853  (6168)                          ELSE
  6854  (6169)                              % Kept for the confirmation, exactly as /tell does.
  6855  (6170)                              typedLen =: lastTellLen
  6856  (6171)                              IF lastTellLen > 76 THEN
  6857  (6172)                                  76 =: lastTellLen
  6858  (6173)                              ENDIF
  6859  (6174)                              FOR i IN 1:lastTellLen DO
  6860  (6175)                                  typed(i - 1) =: lastTell(i - 1)
  6861  (6176)                              ENDFOR
  6862  (6177)  
  6863  (6178)                              buildTellTo(curWin, typedLen) =: length
  6864  (6179)                              sendToServer(length)
  6865  (6180)                          ENDIF
  6866  (6181)                      ELSE
  6867  (6182)                          OUTPUT(1, 'AL20', 'not in the room yet$')
  6868  (6183)                      ENDIF
  6869  (6184)                  ENDIF
  6870  (6185)              ENDIF
  6871  (6186)  
  6872  (6187)              % Cleared only once the finished line has been dealt with, so a
  6873  (6188)              % line still being typed is not thrown away by a drain pass.
  6874  (6189)              IF lineReady THEN
  6875  (6190)                  0 =: typedLen
  6876  (6191)                  TRUE =: inputDirty
  6877  (6192)              ENDIF
  6878  (6193)  
  6879  (6194)              % PUT THE PROMPT BACK WHENEVER SOMETHING HAS BEEN WRITTEN OVER IT -
  6880  (6195)              % a command that has just been dealt with, or lines that arrived
  6881  (6196)              % from the room. Not on an idle pass, or an untouched screen would
  6882  (6197)              % fill with prompts a fifth of a second apart.
  6883  (6198)              % SOMETHING HAPPENED - repaint whichever renderer is in use.
  6884  (6199)              %
  6885  (6200)              % paintChat is the expensive one: 17 rows of 77 characters, about a
  6886  (6201)              % second and a half on a real 9600-baud line. It runs only when a
  6887  (6202)              % message actually arrived or a line was finished, never on an idle
  6888  (6203)              % pass, which is what keeps the loop cheap enough to also poll a
  6889  (6204)              % keyboard.
  6890  (6205)              IF lineReady OR lastDrainCount > 0 THEN
  6891  (6206)                  IF screenOn THEN
  6892  (6207)                      % THE ROOM KEEPS SCROLLING BEHIND AN OPEN WINDOW.
  6893  (6208)                      %
  6894  (6209)                      % This whole block used to sit under "IF NOT panelUp", so a
  6895  (6210)                      % line arriving during /help or /who went into the cache and
  6896  (6211)                      % never onto the screen until the window was closed. That
  6897  (6212)                      % reads as a lost message. Its comment said repainting would
  6898  (6213)                      % draw over the window - true of the paint AS IT WAS, which
  6899  (6214)                      % had no clipping, so the guard was a workaround for the
  6900  (6215)                      % missing rectangle and not a decision about behaviour.
  6901  (6216)                      %
  6902  (6217)                      % paintChat clips now, so it is safe to call with a panel up
  6903  (6218)                      % and the covered strip is simply skipped. The three
  6904  (6219)                      % SINGLE-ROW draws below do NOT clip - each lays out a whole
  6905  (6220)                      % row at once - so each is asked whether the panel is over
  6906  (6221)                      % its row, and closePanel puts back whichever were skipped.
  6907  (6222)                      IF lastDrainCount > 0 THEN
  6908  (6223)                          paintChat
  6909  (6224)  
  6910  (6225)                          % THE BAR CARRIES THE NEWS ABOUT WINDOWS YOU ARE
  6911  (6226)                          % NOT LOOKING AT - a conversation that just
  6912  (6227)                          % opened, an unread count, the star. None of that
  6913  (6228)                          % is on the pane, so an arrival that never redrew
  6914  (6229)                          % the bar left a private message sitting in a
  6915  (6230)                          % window nothing on screen mentioned.
  6916  (6231)                          %
  6917  (6232)                          % Only on a real arrival, and drawBar itself is
  6918  (6233)                          % one short row.
  6919  (6234)                          IF NOT rowCovered(barPRow) THEN
  6920  (6235)                              drawBar
  6921  (6236)                          ENDIF
  6922  (6237)                      ENDIF
  6923  (6238)                      IF NOT rowCovered(statusPRow) THEN
  6924  (6239)                          drawStatus
  6925  (6240)                      ENDIF
  6926  (6241)                      IF NOT rowCovered(inputPRow) THEN
  6927  (6242)                          drawInput
  6928  (6243)                      ENDIF
  6929  (6244)                      flush
  6930  (6245)                  ELSE
  6931  (6246)                      showPrompt
  6932  (6247)                  ENDIF
  6933  (6248)              ENDIF
  6934  (6249)  
  6935  (6250)              % ---- NOTHING HAPPENED? THEN GIVE THE MACHINE A MOMENT -----------
  6936  (6251)              % With the terminal in no-wait, MON1 returns at once whether or not
  6937  (6252)              % anything was typed, so a pass that found no message AND no key is
  6938  (6253)              % a pass that would otherwise go straight round again at full speed.
  6939  (6254)              % That is a SPIN, and it is the same shape that burned the machine
  6940  (6255)              % under CHATSV when XMSG died - the note beside sleepUnits carries
  6941  (6256)              % the measurement.
  6942  (6257)              %
  6943  (6258)              % The sleep is skipped whenever ANYTHING happened, so a busy room
  6944  (6259)              % and a fast typist both stay immediate; only an idle client waits,
  6945  (6260)              % and then only a fifth of a second.
  6946  (6261)              IF lastDrainCount = 0 AND typedLen = 0 AND NOT lineReady THEN
  6947  (6262)                  idleSleep
  6948  (6263)              ELSE
  6949  (6264)                  % SOMETHING HAPPENED, so the next idle pass starts short again.
  6950  (6265)                  % This is the half of the back-off that makes it worth having:
  6951  (6266)                  % without it the client would still be in its long sleep at the
  6952  (6267)                  % exact moment the server's answer to the line just sent lands.
  6953  (6268)                  0 =: idleRuns
  6954  (6269)              ENDIF
  6955  (6270)  
  6956  (6271)              % /quit clears this. The continue-test goes LAST so the leave
  6957  (6272)              % message it sends is on its way before the loop ends.
  6958  (6273)              WHILE running
  6959  (6274)          ENDDO
  6960  (6275)  
  6961  (6276)          % PUT THE TERMINAL BACK. Anything that leaves this loop - /quit, or the
  6962  (6277)          % port going away when XMSG dies - hands the terminal back to the user's
  6963  (6278)          % shell, and it must be handed back in the state it was lent in. A
  6964  (6279)          % terminal left in no-wait is one every later program has to live with.
  6965  (6280)          terminalWait
  6966  (6281)  
  6967  (6282)          % AND ON A SCREEN, THREE MORE THINGS ARE OWED BACK.
  6968  (6283)          %
  6969  (6284)          % The echo, the escape key, and the terminal's own settings - in that
  6970  (6285)          % order, and BEFORE the screen is blanked, because blanking is what
  6971  (6286)          % clears the frame away and anything printed after it belongs to the
  6972  (6287)          % shell.
  6973  (6288)          %
  6974  (6289)          % LEAVING THE ECHO OFF IS THE WORST OF THE THREE. The user is returned
  6975  (6290)          % to a SINTRAN prompt that answers nothing they type, which reads as a
  6976  (6291)          % hung machine and is not one. resetscreen restores terminal STATE, not
  6977  (6292)          % content - blankscreen is what clears the picture.
  6978  (6293)          IF screenOn THEN
  6979  (6294)              MON3(1, 0, echoTab)
  6980  (6295)              MON72(1)
  6981  (6296)              blankscreen
  6982  (6297)              resetscreen
  6983  (6298)          ENDIF
  6984  (6299)      ENDROUTINE
  6985  (6300)  
  6986  (6301)  ENDMODULE
  6987  (6302)  
  6988  (6303)  % NO $EOF HERE, deliberately, and it is not an oversight - an earlier version
  6989  (6304)  % had one and it broke the build.
  6990  (6305)  %
  6991  (6306)  % $EOF belongs at the end of an INCLUDED source, where it stops the compiler
  6992  (6307)  % reading past the file's end and calling that the end of the whole
  6993  (6308)  % compilation. XMPIMP:INCL has one for exactly that reason, and so does the
  6994  (6309)  % shipped (UTILITY)XMSG-PL-VALUES-L:INCL.
  6995  (6310)  %
  6996  (6311)  % In the MAIN source it does something else: it ends the compiler SESSION. The
  6997  (6312)  % compiler links and returns to SINTRAN there and then, so every command after
  6998  (6313)  % the COMPILE - the EXIT, and any LOAD - is handed to SINTRAN instead, which
  6999  (6314)  % answers ILLEGAL COMMAND and aborts the batch job. Measured on D100: the LOAD
  7000  (6315)  % line arrived at the shell as "L".
  7001  (6316)  %
  7002  (6317)  % The library is brought in afterwards by BRF-LINKER-C01. See CHATCC:MODE.
  7003  (6318)  
  7004  (6319)  % ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  7005  (6320)  % WHAT IS NOT FINISHED:
  7006  (6321)  %
  7007  (6322)  %  1. TWO ITEMS THAT USED TO BE ON THIS LIST WERE STALE, and they are recorded
  7008  (6323)  %     here rather than quietly deleted, because a list of open problems that
  7009  (6324)  %     keeps solved ones on it is worse than no list: it sends the next person
  7010  (6325)  %     to work that is already done.
  7011  (6326)  %
  7012  (6327)  %       "Nothing has been compiled."   It compiles and it runs. See the header.
  7013  (6328)  %
  7014  (6329)  %       "No way to leave. kLeave is declared and never sent."   /leave and
  7015  (6330)  %       /quit both send it - handleCommand, which was written after this line
  7016  (6331)  %       and never came back to cross it off.
  7017  (6332)  %
  7018  (6333)  %     The remaining items below were re-read against the code on 2026-08-18 and
  7019  (6334)  %     are still true.
  7020  (6335)  %
  7021  (6336)  %  2. THE LOOP IS A BUSY ONE. It reads the keyboard and the port as fast as the
  7022  (6337)  %     machine will go, which on a shared SINTRAN is antisocial. The server does
  7023  (6338)  %     not have this problem - it BLOCKS on XFWTF - but a client watching two
  7024  (6339)  %     things cannot block on either one.
  7025  (6340)  %
  7026  (6341)  %     THE EXPERIMENT TO RUN, which is one specific thing rather than a research
  7027  (6342)  %     project. Both halves have a documented wake:
  7028  (6343)  %
  7029  (6344)  %       the terminal   after a no-wait call, "SuspendProgram or WaitForRestart
  7030  (6345)  %                      may passivate the program afterwards. The program
  7031  (6346)  %                      restarts when input or output to the device is
  7032  (6347)  %                      completed."          (Monitor Calls, NoWaitSwitch)
  7033  (6348)  %
