  7034  (6349)  %       the port       XMPFRCV with XFWAK and WITHOUT XFWTF returns at once and
  7035  (6350)  %                      ARMS a wake: "the next transmission to this port will
  7036  (6351)  %                      lead to a wake up of the receiver task". Note that "when
  7037  (6352)  %                      the wake up is done, the message is not received, and so
  7038  (6353)  %                      the receiving must be repeated."   (COSMOS guide, XFWAK)
  7039  (6354)  %
  7040  (6355)  %     So the shape to try is: arm the port with XFWAK, then WaitForRestart, and
  7041  (6356)  %     on waking re-issue the receive and re-read the keyboard, because either
  7042  (6357)  %     could be what woke us.
  7043  (6358)  %
  7044  (6359)  %     AND THE OTHER ROUTE IS OPEN AFTER ALL: MAC and NPL are BOTH installed on
  7045  (6360)  %     D100 (verified 2026-08-18 with LIST-REENTRANT, which is the command that
  7046  (6361)  %     answers this - subsystems live in shared memory, not as :PROG files, so
  7047  (6362)  %     LIST-FILES finding nothing proved nothing). So TNOWAIT (307B) can be reached
  7048  (6363)  %     the way the PLANC manual says: an interface routine assembled in MAC and
  7049  (6364)  %     loaded BEFORE PLANC-1BANK-F00. That is now a real option to weigh against the XFWAK
  7050  (6365)  %     experiment below, not a closed door.
  7051  (6366)  %
  7052  (6367)  %     WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED is whether an XMSG wake-up actually restarts a
  7053  (6368)  %     BACKGROUND PROGRAM passivated that way. The call that makes the wake-up
  7054  (6369)  %     context explicit, XMPFWDF, is for DRIVERS - the guide says so, and adds
  7055  (6370)  %     "not permitted for RT-programs". So this is one measurement, not a
  7056  (6371)  %     deduction, and until it is made the loop stays as it is: honest and rude.
  7057  (6372)  %     A short HOLD is the fallback if the wake does not arrive.
  7058  (6373)  %
  7059  (6374)  %  3. THE NAME IS NO LONGER FIXED AT RONNY, and this item is kept in corrected
  7060  (6375)  %     form because it went through a wrong shape on the way.
  7061  (6376)  %
  7062  (6377)  %     It was briefly a PROMPT at start-up, and that was a mistake: on a machine
  7063  (6378)  %     where you have already told SINTRAN who you are by logging in, being
  7064  (6379)  %     asked your name again on every run is nagging. It is now read from
  7065  (6380)  %     CHAT:CNFG in your own file area, changed with /nick, and saved only when
  7066  (6381)  %     the ROOM confirms the rename - never when the request is sent, because
  7067  (6382)  %     the room can refuse it.
  7068  (6383)  %
  7069  (6384)  %     WHAT IS STILL MISSING is the right fallback for somebody who has never
  7070  (6385)  %     renamed: it should be the SINTRAN user name and it is still RONNY.
  7071  (6386)  %     GetUserName is GUSNA 214B, and the PLANC runtime provides no MON214 -
  7072  (6387)  %     the same gap CreateFile 221B falls into, which is why creating CHAT:CNFG
  7073  (6388)  %     goes through MON70 instead. There is no command-level way round 214, so
  7074  (6389)  %     it needs the route the PLANC manual describes in as many words: an
  7075  (6390)  %     interface routine assembled in MAC and loaded BEFORE the PLANC runtime
  7076  (6391)  %     library. MAC is installed here - LIST-REENTRANT shows it - so this is
  7077  (6392)  %     work, not a wall.
  7078  (6393)  %
  7079  (6394)  %  4. The ON ROUTINEERROR form is taken from the Monitor Calls manual's own
  7080  (6395)  %     PLANC example for InByte. That ErrCode 3 specifically means "nothing was
  7081  (6396)  %     typed" is from the NoWaitSwitch entry, which says so for no-wait calls
  7082  (6397)  %     generally rather than for InByte in particular. If some other code turns
  7083  (6398)  %     up in practice, the handler above prints and carries on rather than
  7084  (6399)  %     mistaking it for a keystroke.
  7085  (6400)  % ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
